\caption{\textbf{Cross-arm interface energetics.} Mean per-design potential for five Table-1 GRPO
arms ($n\!\approx\!3800$ designs each, cold A8 sampler) against the Complexa reference under
\emph{two} packings. All five GRPO arms and the \textbf{LMPNN} Complexa column ($n\!=\!3439$) are
re-packed with the \emph{same} LigandMPNN side-chain model, so the packer is not a confound; the
\textbf{nat.\ SC} column ($n\!=\!3500$) keeps Complexa's own generated side chains. \textbf{Bold}
marks the best value among the five GRPO arms in the favorable direction (dir). The bolding exposes
the reward-hack risk: \emph{Base}, the clashiest arm (clash $74$ vs Complexa $\sim\!3$,
$\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}\!=\!+86$), simultaneously \emph{maximizes} the extensive ``good'' terms --- H-bonds,
$\Delta$SASA, packing --- because it wins them by building the biggest, most interpenetrated
interface (iface residues $57$). \emph{expert-ctx s175} instead wins the clean terms
($\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$, clash, BUNS) by \emph{shrinking} the interface (iface $38$, $\Delta$SASA
$1397$). \emph{CHORD s100/s150} are the best-balanced: a native-sized interface (iface $52$--$55$,
$\Delta$SASA $1934$--$2051$) with moderated clash and the best intensive chemistry (electrostatics
$-14.3$, salt bridges $2.3$); s100$\rightarrow$s150 grows the interface slightly and holds the
chemistry --- no energetic erosion. Every GRPO arm still carries $\sim\!5\text{--}28\times$ the
Complexa clash --- consistent with Table \ref{tab:potentials}: clash on the packed set is dead as a
reward and belongs to the backbone / per-token arm (\S3.2, \S3.5). \textbf{The two Complexa columns
answer the packer question:} re-packing Complexa's backbones with \emph{our} LigandMPNN model still
yields a strongly favorable $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}=-27.8$ (vs native-SC $-34.6$) and near-zero clash
($2.6$ vs $4.2$) --- the same regime --- so the $\sim\!40$-unit LJ gap to our best arm is
\emph{intrinsic design headroom in the backbone}, not an artifact of Complexa keeping nicer native
side chains. The final row is a \emph{backbone} metric --- the fraction of designs with $\ge\!1$
broken C--N peptide bond (\ttk{chainbreak\_reward}, count gate) --- computed on the generated
backbone and hence pack-independent: Complexa is essentially break-free ($0.5\%$) while every GRPO
arm sits at $18\text{--}31\%$, and the dense CHORD seqtrack is the \emph{worst} ($31\%$, above base's
$28\%$), so no current reward controls it. This is the mirror of clash: clash is dead on the packed
set (Table \ref{tab:potentials}) but a broken backbone cannot be packed away, so chain-break is the
backbone-integrity term clash cannot be. The relaxed-$\Delta\Delta G$ block (tmol / Rosetta
\ttk{beta\_nov2016}, LMPNN-repack set only; \textbf{nat.\ SC} not scored) Cartesian-minimizes each
bound complex then rigidly separates it. Because uncapped \ttk{fa\_rep} inflates the \emph{raw} mean
on the minority of designs whose interface will not settle ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}$ mean
$107.9$ for Base), the headline $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{iface}}$ is the $10\%$-trimmed mean (within
$<\!1$ kcal of the median); the LJ decomposition ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}$,
$\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}$) and the unrelaxable-clash fraction ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}\!>\!20$)
are reported as means. This block confirms the $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ story with an \emph{orthogonal}
energy function: after local minimization the \emph{typical} design of every arm reaches a
Complexa-grade interface ($-37$ to $-39$ vs LMPNN $-34$), so the large as-generated $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$
penalty is backbone-\emph{reachable} headroom rather than a packing deficit, and the residual
cross-arm gap is pure repulsion (attraction $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}$ is if anything
\emph{stronger} for the arms, $-71$ vs $-44$) concentrated in the clash tail (Base $18\%\!\to\!$
Complexa $0.8\%$). Computed by \ttk{scripts/\_packed\_potentials\_compute.py}
$+$ \ttk{scripts/\_tab6\_energy\_separation.py} (potentials), \ttk{scripts/\_tab6\_chainbreak.py}
(chain-break), and \ttk{scripts/\_tmol\_ddg\_compute.py} $+$ \ttk{scripts/\_tab6\_ddg\_aggregate.py}
(relaxed $\Delta\Delta G$).}
\label{tab:crossarmpot}
\end{table}

\emph{Is $\Delta\Delta G$ just $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}+\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}$?} No. The
interface $\Delta\Delta G$ is the sum of the \emph{six} cross-chain (two-body) terms of
\ttk{beta\_nov2016}, each scored as (bound complex) $-$ (rigidly separated chains):
\[
\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{iface}}
= \underbrace{\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}+\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}}_{\text{Lennard--Jones (vdW)}}
\;+\; \underbrace{\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{sol}}+\Delta\Delta G_{\text{lk-ball}}}_{\text{solvation}}
\;+\; \Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{elec}}
\;+\; \Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{Hb}} .
\]
The pair $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}+\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}$ is therefore \emph{only} the van
der Waals (Lennard--Jones) contribution --- the Rosetta counterpart of our bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$
--- while the remaining four terms carry solvation ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{sol}}$,
$\Delta\Delta G_{\text{lk-ball}}$), electrostatics ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{elec}}$) and hydrogen
bonding ($\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{Hb}}$). The one-body / intramolecular terms (\ttk{fa\_dun},
\ttk{rama}, \ttk{p\_aa\_pp}, \ttk{ref}, $\dots$) do \emph{not} enter: they cancel in the rigid
interface difference (the same monomer internal energy is present bound and separated) --- which is
also why the full-scorefunction \ttk{ddg\_total}, that re-admits them once one-sided relaxation breaks
the cancellation, is \emph{not} used here. Numerically the LJ pair dominates: for Complexa the non-LJ
remainder nearly vanishes ($21.7-1.5-14.7-5.4 = +0.1$, so $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{iface}}\approx
\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{atr}}+\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}} = -38.7$), whereas for the clashy Base arm
it adds $+16.6$ on top of the LJ $+37.0$. The identity is \emph{exact per design}, and hence for the
column \emph{means} by linearity ($-70.9+107.9+43.3-3.7-17.0-6.0 = +53.5$ for Base); it does
\emph{not} hold for the trimmed/median headline, because order statistics are nonlinear. That is
exactly why the table reports the robust trim-$10\%$ $\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{iface}}$ (the typical
design) \emph{separately} from the additive per-term means --- the mean
$\Delta\Delta G_{\mathrm{rep}}$, and thus the mean total, is dominated by the unrelaxable-clash tail
that trimming removes.

\emph{Reward-design consequence.} Table \ref{tab:crossarmpot} sharpens the integration in
\S\ref{sec:pertoken-pot}. The two extensive ``area'' terms --- interface H-bonds and buried
$\Delta$SASA --- are \emph{maximized by the clashiest arm} (Base) and by the longest CHORD rollout
(s150), so grading their raw sums would reward interpenetration and interface bloat, the exact
failure the bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ was built to resist. They are therefore graded only through
their per-atom \emph{density} companions (\S\ref{sec:pertoken-pot}), or demoted to tracked
diagnostics. The bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ is the primary graded term: its sign is unambiguous
(both Complexa packings $\approx\!-30$ vs all five arms positive), it cannot be won by
interpenetration, and no arm has reached a favorable value --- the uniform-repack column proves the
$\sim\!40$-unit gap is real backbone headroom, not packing, so the reward carries genuine,
non-saturated gradient; the relaxed tmol $\Delta\Delta G$ rows further show this headroom is
physically \emph{reachable} --- local backbone minimization already attains Complexa-grade
$\Delta\Delta G$ --- so the gradient points at an achievable target, not an intrinsic limit. The
intensive chemistry terms (electrostatics, salt bridges), where CHORD
already edges past both Complexa packings, are the low-risk secondary rewards. This is the direct
evidence for grading $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ (density-normalized) as the primary second-set reward with
$\R_{\Delta\mathrm{SASA}}$/$\R_{\mathrm{HB}}$ entering only in density form. Finally, the chain-break
row identifies a \emph{backbone} bucket the interface potentials cannot cover: no arm controls
peptide-bond integrity (CHORD is worst at $31\%$ vs Complexa's $0.5\%$), so the next run pairs the
packed interface-energy set with a backbone / per-token structure set --- per-token clash \emph{and}
a graded chain-break term (\ttk{chainbreak\_reward}, \S3.2/\S3.5) --- since chain-break survives the
pack exactly where clash does not.

\subsection{The tmol interface-\texorpdfstring{$\Delta\Delta G$}{ddG} upgrade: a matched head-to-head}
\label{sec:ddg}
The bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ of Table \ref{tab:potentials} is a hand-rolled soft-core Lennard-Jones
sum. A physically fuller substitute is a genuine \emph{interface} $\Delta\Delta G$ from
\ttk{tmol} (a GPU/CPU PyTorch reimplementation of Rosetta's \ttk{beta\_nov2016}): it adds the
isotropic Lennard--Kossmann desolvation term (\ttk{fa\_lk}, the physics the numpy LJ lacks entirely),
an orientation-dependent H-bond potential, \ttk{lk\_ball} anisotropic solvation, and screened
electrostatics --- and, crucially, it can \emph{relax} the structure by Cartesian LBFGS minimization
before scoring, so the number reports a settled energy rather than the raw generated coordinates. We
built this reward and packaged it (\S\ref{sec:package} status), in two flavors: \emph{rigid}
(\ttk{ddg\_*}: relaxed-bound complex minus the rigidly-separated partners --- the pure cross-chain
interaction energy) and \emph{unbound-relaxed} (\ttk{ddg\_ub\_*}: each separated partner
independently re-minimized, so the number also credits strain relief on unbinding, the
InterfaceAnalyzer convention). The gate from the scope doc is explicit: \emph{wire the $\Delta\Delta G$
term with positive weight only if a flavor beats the numpy $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ it would replace.}

\emph{Matched discriminator (offline).} We scored $811$ A8 cold-generated designs from the current
best GRPO checkpoint ($32$ targets, $111$ pass $/\,700$ fail, $13.7\%$) \emph{two ways on the
identical set}: the tmol $\Delta\Delta G$ (both flavors, each with two Cartesian minimizations;
$0$ errors on all $811$) and the numpy packed potentials with SASA. Table \ref{tab:ddg} ranks every
term by AUROC against the same Protenix pass label. The result is a clean \emph{null}: on this set
\textbf{every interface-energy term --- numpy and tmol alike --- collapses toward random}. The numpy
$\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ here is $0.529$, \emph{not} the $0.778$ of Table \ref{tab:potentials}; the best
tmol total (\ttk{ddg\_ub\_total}, $0.537$) edges it by $0.008$, but with $111$ positives the AUROC
standard error is $\approx\!0.047$, so that gap is $\approx\!0.17$ SE --- noise --- and the whole
panel ($0.500$--$0.564$) sits within $\sim\!1$ SE of $0.5$. The nominal leaders (numpy \ttk{e\_elec}
$0.564$, tmol \ttk{ddg\_hb} $0.546$) are not exceptions to this; they are inside the same noise band.

                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{1.2cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{2.2cm}@{}}

fall in $[0.500,0.525]$. Highlighted rows are the two totals whose comparison the gate turns on:
$\Delta\Delta G$ ($0.537$) versus the numpy bounded LJ ($0.529$) --- a $0.008$ gap against an AUROC
SE of $\approx\!0.047$. Computed by \ttk{scripts/\_tmol\_ddg\_compute.py} $+$

\label{tab:ddg}

\emph{Why the null, and the decision.} The discrepancy with Table \ref{tab:potentials}'s $0.778$ is
the \emph{pool}, not the metric. Table \ref{tab:potentials} spanned $3500$ designs across the whole
base$\rightarrow$GRPO trajectory --- a wide interface-energy range, from clashy early designs to
de-clashed late ones --- where LJ energy is a strong pass-predictor precisely because it separates
those regimes. The A8 set is a single advanced checkpoint's cold generations: interface energy is
already compressed and near-native, so within it pass/fail turns on fold/dock correctness, not on
residual interaction energy, and \emph{no} energy term --- our numpy $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ included ---
can separate the band. This matched null therefore does \emph{not} clear the gate: the physics
upgrade shows no decisive AUROC advantage over the numpy $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ it would replace, at
$\sim\!30$--$60\times$ the per-design cost (two Cartesian minimizations). \textbf{The tmol
$\Delta\Delta G$ ships at weight $0$} --- packaged, wired-ready, and computed as a tracked diagnostic
--- rather than as a graded reward. It is a drop-in the moment a wide-pool re-test (the
base$\rightarrow$GRPO span of Table \ref{tab:potentials}, where $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ reaches $0.778$)
shows a $\Delta\Delta G$ flavor separating \emph{that} regime better than the numpy sum; on a matched
within-checkpoint set the two are statistically indistinguishable, so the cheaper term stays primary.

\subsection{Matched head-to-head: per-token vs.\ per-design \texorpdfstring{$e_{lj}$}{e\_lj} credit (steps 175 and 725)}
\label{sec:elj-granularity}
The dense-vs-sparse construction of \S\ref{sec:pertoken-pot} and Fig.\ \ref{fig:elj-compute} isolates a
single variable --- how the \emph{identical} bounded-LJ signal is delivered into the advantage:
\textbf{dense} splits $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ per binder residue and lands it on the structure track
(\ttk{per\_token\_pot}, \ttk{w\_pt\_lj}$=1$); \textbf{sparse} $z$-scores each design's \emph{total}
$e_{lj}$ within the rollout group and adds it to the design-level advantage (\ttk{w\_elj\_scalar}$=1$).
Both carry an identical supporting stack (smooth clash $+$ interface-contact band, chainbreak, and the
four $\mathrm{LC}$/Jaccard diversity terms), an identical warm start (\ttk{leflur-binder-3di}), group
size $64$, and step schedule; a matched \textbf{no-$e_{lj}$ baseline} runs the supporting stack alone.
Table \ref{tab:eljgranularity} reads all three arms at the early step $175$ and, for the two $e_{lj}$
arms, again at the later common checkpoint step $725$, over the Table-6 metric suite (cold A8 sampler,
$380$ designs $=10\times38$ targets), with the packed interface $e_{lj}$ --- the graded signal itself
--- added at top.

\emph{Result.} The \textbf{per-design scalar wins on the quantity it optimizes}: mean interface
$e_{lj}$ falls to $9.3$ (median $-5.9$), below the no-$e_{lj}$ baseline ($16.1$) and far below the
per-token arm ($57.5$). It also posts the lowest backbone clash ($E_{\mathrm{clash}}=7.1$) and is the
only arm to pull the interface fraction into the native band ($0.164$ vs.\ native $0.14$--$0.16$), all
\emph{without} the sequence-quality regression the per-token arm suffers (AAR $0.260$, matching both
the baseline and the base policy, vs.\ dense $0.188$). \textbf{The per-token structure-track credit is
counterproductive here}: it \emph{raises} packed $e_{lj}$ above doing nothing and drops pass\%, AAR,
and $C_{\mathrm{mpnn}}$ --- the group-relative per-residue credit moves the structure tokens in a
direction that does not reduce the packed interaction energy. Its only advantages are secondary
(least-degenerate $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$, fewest chain breaks, and --- on the \emph{tmol}
$\Delta\Delta G$ flavor, not the numpy $e_{lj}$ --- the lowest $\Delta\Delta G$). This inverts the
usual ``denser credit is better'' intuition for this particular signal. At step $175$ pass\%
($4.74/3.95/2.89$ for none/sparse/dense) still trails the base A8 policy ($5.61$), so the energetic
and sequence-quality separations --- not pass\% --- are the load-bearing read there.

\emph{The separation sharpens by step $725$.} Trained $\sim\!4\times$ longer, the per-design scalar
drives interface $e_{lj}$ \emph{net-favorable} (mean $-4.0$, median $-10.9$) --- below every step-175
arm and far below the per-token arm, which only reaches $22.8$ (median $-0.8$), still worse than doing
nothing at step $175$. Sparse now leads dense on $11$ of $15$ Table-6 columns: $\sim\!3\times$ the pass\%
($2.37$ vs.\ $0.79$), higher AAR ($0.240$ vs.\ $0.180$), lower $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$ ($4.5$ vs.\ $8.0$),
fewer breaks ($15.0$ vs.\ $20.5$), and markedly healthier sequences ($\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$ $0.622$
vs.\ $0.469$ --- the per-token arm is drifting toward degeneracy). Dense keeps only the same secondary
wins it held at $175$ ($3$Di TV, $R_g$, tmol $\Delta\Delta G$), all consistent with proxy-chasing rather
than a better interface. \textbf{Caveats:} (i) both $e_{lj}$ arms still trail the base A8 pass\%
($5.61$) --- the reward is minimizing its proxies (clash, break\%, $e_{lj}$) faster than pass\% recovers;
and (ii) $R_g$ stays high on both arms ($16$--$17$\,\AA\ vs.\ native $\sim\!12.5$) --- the binders are
not compacting, which is exactly the motivation for the sparse-$+R_g$ follow-up of \S\ref{sec:elj-rog}
(Table \ref{tab:eljrog}), which does pull $R_g$ down to $14.7$\,\AA.
The head-to-head verdict is nonetheless clear: \textbf{for the bounded-LJ signal the per-design scalar
dominates the per-token decomposition, and the gap widens with training}.

\subsection{Adding a compactness reward on top of the winner: sparse \texorpdfstring{$e_{lj}+R_g$}{e\_lj + Rg}}
\label{sec:elj-rog}
The one failure axis every $e_{lj}$ arm above leaves unfixed is $R_g$: the binders de-clash and reach
the native interface fraction but stay over-extended ($16$--$17$\,\AA\ vs.\ native $\sim\!12.5$). The
follow-up arm keeps the winning per-design sparse $e_{lj}$ term \emph{byte-for-byte} and adds a single
new variable --- a per-design saturating compactness reward on the binder radius of gyration
(\ttk{w\_rog}$=1$, on the length-normalized compactness $2.2\,n^{1/3}/R_g$). At step $300$ it
\textbf{compacts as intended}: $R_g$ falls to $14.7$\,\AA, below the no-$R_g$ sparse arm at
\emph{both} available checkpoints ($17.4$ at $175$, $16.4$ at $725$) and moving toward native. It is
also the \textbf{best-passing and highest-AAR $e_{lj}$ arm so far} --- pass\% $3.95$ (vs.\ sparse
$2.37$ / dense $0.79$ at step $725$) and AAR $0.296$, itself above the base A8 policy ($0.255$) --- while
holding backbone clash low ($E_{\mathrm{clash}}=8.2$) and the interface fraction near-native ($0.175$);
Table \ref{tab:eljrog}. \textbf{Caveats:} it still trails the base A8 pass\% ($5.61$); the interface
remains low-confidence (ipSAE $0.054$); and $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$ ($0.436$) has drifted below base
--- so the $R_g$ term buys fold-state realism without yet closing the pass\% gap. This is a step-$300$
read, \emph{not} step-matched to a no-$R_g$ arm at $300$: the compaction is a within-run trajectory
measured against the $175$/$725$ no-$R_g$ anchors.

\paragraph{Trajectory of the sparse $e_{lj}+R_g$ arm across checkpoints.}
The compaction the $R_g$ term buys is durable, but the arm \emph{peaks early and then
over-optimizes}. Table \ref{tab:eljrogtraj} tracks the same run at the two checkpoints evaluated so
far --- step $300$ and step $750$. The targeted variable holds: $R_g$ stays at $14.7$\,\AA\ at both
steps (native $\sim\!12.5$), and every proxy the reward stack directly touches keeps improving from
$300$ to $750$ --- interface LJ falls from $e_{lj}=9.0$ to $\mathbf{-3.8}$ (repulsive $\to$
attractive), $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$ $8.2\!\to\!7.3$, 3Di TV $0.285\!\to\!0.274$, SC $0.917\!\to\!0.923$.
\emph{Yet the design-quality metrics the reward does not see erode over the same interval}: pass\%
$3.95\!\to\!1.84$, AAR $0.296\!\to\!0.236$, and interface $\Delta\Delta G$ $-32.9\!\to\!-30.0$. This
is the signature reward-hacking split --- the checkpoint that scores its own reward best is
\emph{not} the checkpoint that designs best --- and it is the same learn-then-diverge pattern seen in
the distribution-reward runs. Operationally: \textbf{keep the $\sim\!300$ checkpoint, not $750$};
later steps trade real fold/dock quality for proxy gains. Neither checkpoint yet reaches the base A8
pass\% ($5.61$), but step $300$ remains the strongest $e_{lj}$-family arm to date.

\subsection{Interface biochemical complementarity: REF2015 desolvation (\ttk{fa\_sol}) and electrostatics (\ttk{fa\_elec}) first}
\label{sec:biochem-complementarity}
The bounded LJ reward of \S\ref{sec:potentials} grades the \emph{packing} of the interface, and the
offline gate (Table \ref{tab:potentials}) confirms it is the single most predictive packed potential
($\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$, AUROC $0.778$). But the same gate exposes where a pure-LJ score is blind to
\emph{chemistry}: the shipped H-bond term is a distance-only count ($0.742$) that cannot tell a
geometrically real donor$\rightarrow$acceptor contact from an incidentally close polar pair, and raw
Coulomb electrostatics is nearly dead ($0.549$). A natural hypothesis is that the diagnostic is dead
only because it uses a \emph{constant} dielectric and so lacks the distance-dependent screening a real
force field applies; we test that below by gating the byte-faithful Rosetta \ttk{fa\_elec}, and (spoiler)
the screening does \emph{not} rescue it (\S\ref{fig:faelec}, AUROC $0.502$ --- chance). The natural next
reward --- and the one requested here --- is a \emph{single interface complementarity score that sums
the per-pair energetics of every interaction across the interface}: favorable hydrophobic burial,
distance-gated electrostatic and salt-bridge contacts, and \emph{orientation-dependent} hydrogen
bonds, so that the reward measures whether the two surfaces are biochemically complementary, not
merely space-filling.

Rather than invent a scoring function, we reimplement the physically-motivated, distance-gated
pairwise terms of the \textbf{Rosetta REF2015 all-atom energy function} (Alford et al.\ 2017
\cite{alford2017}) --- the most heavily validated and widely cited protein energy function, and one
whose terms are individually documented closed forms we can port to NumPy against the \emph{same}
LigandMPNN pack the LJ reward already consumes (no PyRosetta at train time; \ttk{tmol}, already
installed for the $\Delta\Delta G$ upgrade of \S\ref{sec:tmol}, is a drop-in PyTorch reference).
Restricting REF2015 to cross-interface atom pairs $(i,j)\in\mathcal I$ ($i\in$ antigen, $j\in$
binder), the interface complementarity energy is the weighted linear combination
\begin{equation}
E_{\mathrm{iface}}=\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal I}\!\Big[
   w_{\mathrm{atr}}\,\underbrace{E^{ij}_{\mathrm{atr}}}_{\text{vdW attr.}}
  +w_{\mathrm{rep}}\,\underbrace{E^{ij}_{\mathrm{rep}}}_{\text{vdW rep.}}
  +w_{\mathrm{sol}}\,\underbrace{E^{ij}_{\mathrm{sol}}}_{\text{desolv.}}
  +w_{\mathrm{elec}}\,\underbrace{E^{ij}_{\mathrm{elec}}}_{\text{Coulomb}}\Big]
  \;+\!\!\sum_{(d,a)\in\mathcal I}\!\! w_{\mathrm{hb}}\,\underbrace{E^{da}_{\mathrm{hb}}}_{\text{orient.\ H-bond}},
\label{eq:eiface}
\end{equation}
with the REF2015 default weights (Alford et al.\ 2017, Table 1): $w_{\mathrm{atr}}{=}1.0$,
$w_{\mathrm{rep}}{=}0.55$, $w_{\mathrm{sol}}{=}1.0$, $w_{\mathrm{elec}}{=}1.0$, $w_{\mathrm{hb}}{=}1.0$
(all four backbone/side-chain H-bond channels). The score is a \emph{penalty} in kcal/mol (lower $=$
more complementary); the reward is $\R_{\mathrm{bio}}=-E_{\mathrm{iface}}$, delivered either as the
sparse per-design group $z$-score that won \S\ref{sec:elj-granularity} or --- since every term is an
additive sum over pairs --- as the exact per-residue decomposition of \S\ref{sec:pertoken-pot}.

\paragraph{Two chemistry terms first.} The van der Waals piece ($E_{\mathrm{atr}},E_{\mathrm{rep}}$)
is already shipped as the bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ of Fig.\ \ref{fig:elj-compute} and needs no new
code. Of the remaining three, the orientation-dependent hydrogen-bond term is by far the most
involved to reimplement --- it requires explicit donor/acceptor typing, hydrogen placement, four
measured angles and torsions, and the fitted REF2015 splines --- so we defer it (\emph{phase 2},
below). \textbf{For the initial experiments we implement the two atom-pair chemistry terms that
$e_{lj}$ is blindest to and that need only interatomic distances and per-atom-type constants:
Lazaridis--Karplus desolvation $\R_{\mathrm{sol}}$ (\ttk{fa\_sol}) and dielectric-screened
electrostatics $\R_{\mathrm{elec}}$ (\ttk{fa\_elec}).} Each is a self-normalized per-design reward on
the \emph{same} LigandMPNN pack the $e_{lj}$ arm already consumes, gated on the same $3500$-design set
before wiring (Table \ref{tab:potentials}), and added on top of the winning sparse $e_{lj}$ arm as a
single controlled variable, so their checkpoints compare step-for-step against
\S\ref{sec:elj-granularity}.

\paragraph{Solvation reward $\R_{\mathrm{sol}}$ (\ttk{fa\_sol}).} Desolvation is a per-pair
\emph{Gaussian-exclusion} penalty (Lazaridis \& Karplus 1999 \cite{lk1999}): when binder atom $j$
enters the first solvation shell of antigen atom $i$ it displaces the ordered water $i$ had paid to
organize, and the lost solvation free energy is charged as
\begin{equation}
E^{i\leftarrow j}_{\mathrm{sol}}=-\frac{V_j\,\Delta G^{\mathrm{free}}_i}
   {2\pi^{3/2}\lambda_i\,d_{ij}^{2}}\,
   \exp\!\Big[-\big((d_{ij}-\R_i)/\lambda_i\big)^{2}\Big],
\qquad
E_{\mathrm{sol}}=\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal I}\!\big(E^{i\leftarrow j}_{\mathrm{sol}}+E^{j\leftarrow i}_{\mathrm{sol}}\big),
\label{eq:fasol}
\end{equation}
summed in both directions (each atom desolvates the other) and over all interface pairs. This is the
form in the reference code (\ttk{tmol}/Rosetta \ttk{lk\_isotropic}), which differs from the
\emph{printed} Eq.~11 of Alford et al.\ 2017 \cite{alford2017} in two transcriptions: the $1/d^2$
falloff is on the \emph{pair distance} $d_{ij}$ (not $\sigma_i^2$), and the Gaussian is centered on the
desolvated atom's \emph{own} LJ radius $\R_i{=}r_i$ (not the summed $\sigma_{ij}$) --- both matching the
original Lazaridis--Karplus 1999 derivation \cite{lk1999}. The magnitude is set by the displacing
neighbor's atomic volume $V_j$ and the reference desolvation free energy $\Delta G^{\mathrm{free}}_i$ of
the atom being desolvated; the Gaussian decays over the correlation length $\lambda_i$, which is a
\emph{uniform} $3.5$\,\AA\ for every protein atom type in \ttk{beta\_nov2016}. The pair term is held
flat below $d_{\min}{=}0.89\,\sigma_{ij}$ (with carbon--carbon contacts floored at $4.2$\,\AA, Rosetta's
\ttk{modifypot} linearization), cubic-splined across the near-contact bins, and cubic-tapered to zero
from $4.5$ to $6.0$\,\AA\ (Fig.\ \ref{fig:fasol}); $\sigma_{ij}{=}r_i{+}r_j$ (with donor--acceptor
H-bond overrides, $3.0$/$2.6$\,\AA) enters \emph{only} through this flatten threshold and the spline
knots, never the Gaussian. Following Rosetta, the LK reference (self) term is omitted --- the energy
function carries its own reference energy. The term is \emph{signed}: $|\Delta G^{\mathrm{free}}|$ is
large and negative for polar/charged atoms, so burying an unsatisfied polar atom is a
\emph{penalty} --- a cost worth paying only if an H-bond or salt bridge repays it (\ttk{fa\_sol} itself
credits no H-bond) --- while $\Delta G^{\mathrm{free}}{>}0$ for aliphatic carbon makes carbon burial
slightly \emph{favorable} (negative), so the term rewards hydrophobic packing as well as penalizing
exposed-polar burial. It depends only on the pair distance and per-atom-type constants, taken verbatim
from the \ttk{tmol}/Rosetta \ttk{beta\_nov2016} database; the reward is $\R_{\mathrm{sol}}=-E_{\mathrm{sol}}$.

own reference energy). Reimplemented in \ttk{scripts/\_fasol.py} and validated against the compiled
\ttk{tmol} \ttk{LKScore} to $|\Delta|<10^{-7}$\,kcal/mol over ten representative atom-type pairs.
$E_{\mathrm{sol}}$ enters $E_{\mathrm{iface}}$ as a penalty; the reward is
$\R_{\mathrm{sol}}=-E_{\mathrm{sol}}$.}

\paragraph{Offline gate for $\R_{\mathrm{sol}}$: real physics, but normalize to interface size.} We
ran \ttk{fa\_sol} through the same discrimination gate as every other candidate reward, on the
high--success--rate Complexa pack ($3439$ LigandMPNN-packed designs, $28.7\%$ Protenix-pass, $35$
targets --- a balanced-label pool, not the $3500$-design span of Table
\ref{tab:potentials}). The naive read looks weak: raw \ttk{fa\_sol} scores AUROC $0.594$, far below
$\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$'s $0.720$ on this pool, and with the \emph{wrong} apparent sign. The reason is
that \ttk{fa\_sol} is a \emph{total} summed over interface pairs, so it scales with how much
interface is buried: it correlates $+0.85$ with the interface atom count and $+0.84$ with the
interface residue count (but only $+0.03$ with binder length --- it tracks the docked contact patch,
not the chain). A larger interface both accrues more desolvation penalty and passes more often, so
the size confound leaks through the raw score with the wrong orientation. Once interface size is
removed --- either by evaluating \emph{within} a target (same antigen, size varying by pose) or by
normalizing per interface atom --- the picture inverts to the physically correct one and the true,
modest design-level signal appears (Table \ref{tab:fasolnorm}): the unnormalized total collapses to
chance ($0.491$), while the size-normalized \ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_atom} and \ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_res}
recover to $0.528$ (their correlation with interface size falls $+0.85\!\rightarrow\!+0.20$). Under
target-stratified GroupKFold controlling for interface size, the orthogonal design-level lift of
\ttk{fa\_sol} \emph{over} $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ is only $+0.008$ (and $-0.005$ for the per-atom variant);
the larger pooled lift ($+0.03$ to $+0.05$) is cross-target confounding --- it ranks easy vs.\ hard
targets, not designs within a target.

\ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_atom} ($/\,n_{\mathrm{iface\,atoms}}$) & $0.528$ & $+0.20$\\
\rowcolor{aTint}\ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_res} ($/\,n_{\mathrm{iface\,res}}$)\ \textbf{[train default]}  & $0.528$ & $+0.20$\\
\midrule
$\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ (\ttk{e\_lj}, total, for reference)  & $0.571$ & ---\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{\textbf{\ttk{fa\_sol} must be normalized to interface size to carry design-level signal.}
Mean within-target AUROC vs.\ the Protenix pass label on the $3439$-design Complexa pack ($32$
targets with $\ge3$ pass and $\ge3$ fail). The raw total is a near-proxy for interface size
($r{=}+0.85$) and is uninformative once that is removed ($0.491$); dividing by interface atom or
residue count restores a weak-but-real signal ($0.528$). \ttk{fa\_sol} correlates $-0.54$ with
\ttk{e\_lj}, so it is not fully redundant, but its marginal design-level lift over \ttk{e\_lj} is
small ($+0.008$).}
\label{tab:fasolnorm}
\end{table}

\emph{Decision.} Lazaridis--Karplus desolvation is a genuine component of the binding free energy
and is only partly redundant with $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ (they correlate $-0.54$), so we keep it --- but
at training time we default to the \emph{per-residue} normalization \ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_res}
($\R_{\mathrm{sol}}=-E_{\mathrm{sol}}/n_{\mathrm{iface\,res}}$), not the total, and at a \emph{low}
weight as a companion to the primary $e_{lj}$ reward. Delivering the unnormalized total into the group
z-scored advantage would mostly reward inflating the contact patch (its $+0.85$ size correlation),
a hacking vector we have seen before; the per-residue form ($+0.20$) rewards \emph{better} desolvation
per interface residue instead. Per-residue and per-atom are equally predictive within a target
($0.528$ each), and per-residue matches the normalization of the other interface-density companions
(\ttk{hb\_density}, \ttk{buns\_density}), which already divide by $n_{\mathrm{iface\,res}}$ --- so it
is the consistent default. The port itself is exact regardless of this choice --- validated byte-for-byte
against the compiled \ttk{tmol} \ttk{LKScore} to $|\Delta|<10^{-7}$\,kcal/mol (Fig.\ \ref{fig:fasol}).

\paragraph{Eval verdict (step 300): the gradient works, the reward is hackable.} We launched the
\ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_res} companion on top of the sparse $e_{lj}+R_g$ winner ($w_{\mathrm{fasol}}{=}0.5$)
and evaluated it at step $300$ against the identical arm \emph{without} \ttk{fa\_sol} (the sparse
$e_{lj}+R_g$ reference, same sampler and seeds). Crucially, we measured the newly added reward term
\emph{on the arm's own generations} --- the only way to tell whether the model actually optimized it.
It did, decisively: \ttk{fa\_sol} falls $49.4\!\to\!26.2$ (a $\sim\!9\sigma$ separation over $380$
designs), \ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_atom} $0.143\!\to\!0.085$ (Table \ref{tab:fasoleval}, top panel). The
GRPO gradient flowed and drove the reward hard. But the model minimized desolvation by the wrong
route --- \emph{retracting the interface} rather than packing it better: every companion interface
term degrades in lockstep --- attractive LJ \ttk{e\_lj\_atr} $-65.8\!\to\!-48.7$ (less attraction),
hydrogen bonds \ttk{n\_hb} $8.9\!\to\!4.6$ (halved), salt bridges \ttk{n\_saltbridge} $1.41\!\to\!0.25$
(all but gone), and electrostatics \ttk{e\_elec} $-3.30\!\to\!-0.70$. Fewer buried polar atoms is
lower desolvation \emph{and} a smaller, weaker, non-functional interface. The design-quality metrics
follow it down (bottom panel): pass\% collapses $3.95\!\to\!1.32$, sequence entropy $H$ $3.41\!\to\!2.71$
and $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$ $0.436\!\to\!0.261$ (severe degeneracy), and $R_g$ \emph{de}-compacts
$14.7\!\to\!16.4$ --- \ttk{fa\_sol} actively fought the $R_g$ term. This is the cleanest confirmation of
the offline gate's warning (Table \ref{tab:fasolnorm}): the problem was never gradient flow, it was
\emph{reward quality} --- \ttk{fa\_sol} (raw AUROC $0.594$, orthogonal lift over $e_{lj}$ only $+0.008$)
is easier to hack by interface retraction than to satisfy honestly. \textbf{\ttk{fa\_sol} is dead as a
reward on this stack}; it is retained only as a diagnostic. The methodological lesson is baked into the
eval discipline: when an arm adds a new reward term, always measure that term on the arm's own gens
next to a reference arm, to separate ``the gradient never flowed'' from ``the gradient flowed but the
reward was hackable'' --- the two are indistinguishable if one only watches pass\%.

\paragraph{Electrostatics reward $\R_{\mathrm{elec}}$ (\ttk{fa\_elec}).} Electrostatics is Coulomb's
law between charged interface atoms, but with a \emph{distance-dependent} dielectric (Rosetta REF2015
\ttk{fa\_elec}; Alford et al.\ 2017 \cite{alford2017}). The Coulombic-region pair energy is
\begin{equation}
E_{\mathrm{elec}}(i,j)=q_i\,q_j\!\left[\frac{C_1}{\epsilon(d_{ij})\,d_{ij}}-C_2\right],
\qquad
\epsilon(d)=D-\tfrac{1}{2}(D-D_0)\bigl(2+2dS+d^2S^2\bigr)e^{-dS},
\label{eq:faelec}
\end{equation}
with $C_1=322.0637\ \text{\AA}\cdot\text{kcal}\cdot\text{mol}^{-1}\!\cdot\text{e}^{-2}$, the shift
$C_2=C_1/(d_{\max}\,\epsilon(d_{\max}))$ that makes the energy vanish at the cutoff, and Rosetta's
global constants $D=79.931$, $D_0=6.648$, $S=0.4415\,\text{\AA}^{-1}$, $d_{\min}=1.6$\,\AA,
$d_{\max}=5.5$\,\AA. The interface energy $E_{\mathrm{elec}}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal I}E_{\mathrm{elec}}(i,j)$
is a five-region piecewise potential: Eq.\ \ref{eq:faelec} holds only in the Coulombic middle
($d_{\min}{+}0.25\le d<d_{\max}{-}1.0$); below $d_{\min}{-}0.25=1.35$\,\AA\ it is held \emph{flat} at
the finite cap $q_iq_j[C_1/(d_{\min}\epsilon(d_{\min}))-C_2]$, and cubic-Hermite splines join the cap
to the Coulombic form over $1.35$--$1.85$\,\AA\ and taper it to zero over $4.5$--$5.5$\,\AA. The
dielectric $\epsilon(d)$ is a smooth sigmoid rising from $\epsilon(0)=D_0=6.648$ at contact to
$\epsilon(\infty)=D=79.931$ in bulk (Fig.\ \ref{fig:faelec}, inset), so screening \emph{grows with
separation} --- a close pair sits at low dielectric and reads strong, while a distant
charge pair is screened toward the bulk value and reads weak. \emph{Crucially, $\epsilon$ depends on the
pair distance alone}: burial is modeled only \emph{implicitly}, through the built-in assumption that a
short contact sits in a protein-like, weakly-screened environment and a long one in bulk water. There
is no solvent-accessibility or burial term in the potential --- a solvent-exposed salt bridge and a
deeply buried one at the same separation receive the \emph{identical} dielectric (e.g.\
$\epsilon(1.6\,\text{\AA}){=}9.2$, $\epsilon(3\,\text{\AA}){=}17.5$, $\epsilon(5.5\,\text{\AA}){=}38.7$,
reaching the bulk $D{=}79.9$ only asymptotically). This distance-as-burial proxy is a known
approximation of REF2015 \ttk{fa\_elec} (it buys a cheap, differentiable, pairwise closed form; a
true burial dependence would require a SASA-scaled dielectric or a generalized-Born term), and it is
the leading suspect for the offline-gate result below. Opposite charges give a favorable ($<0$)
contribution and like charges an unfavorable one; the flat cap below $1.35$\,\AA\ (the electrostatic
analogue of the LJ repulsion cap) means an overlap cannot forge an infinite favorable energy. Because
our packed complexes are heavy-atom-only (hydrogens stripped), $q_i$ are the net/sign-correct
\emph{formal charged-group charges} (Asp/Glu carboxylate O, Lys N$\zeta$, Arg guanidinium N, His N ---
the same \ttk{CHARGE} table as the crude diagnostic), not H-dependent CHARMM partial charges. The
functional form is a dependency-light NumPy port (\ttk{scripts/\_faelec.py}) validated byte-for-byte
against the compiled \ttk{tmol} \ttk{elec()} (\ttk{scripts/\_faelec\_validate.py}). We
\emph{hypothesized} that this distance-dependent screening would recover the signal our raw-Coulomb
diagnostic (a \emph{constant} dielectric; AUROC $0.549$, Table \ref{tab:potentials}) lacked --- by
discounting the long-range charge noise that drowned it and up-weighting the close, geometrically real
salt bridges. The offline gate below tests that hypothesis directly. If used, the reward would be
$\R_{\mathrm{elec}}=-E_{\mathrm{elec}}$.

\centering

$\epsilon(d)$ that rises smoothly from $\epsilon(0)=D_0=6.648$ at contact to $\epsilon(\infty)=D=79.931$
in bulk (inset). Screening therefore grows with separation: a close, buried salt bridge sits at low
dielectric and reads strong, while a distant charge pair is screened toward the bulk value and reads
weak --- the screened potential (blue, main panel) is far shallower than a constant-dielectric bare
$1/d$ (grey). The energy is held constant below $d_{\min}{-}0.25=1.35$\,\AA\ (an overlap cannot forge
an infinite favorable energy) and shifted by $C_2$ to reach zero at the $5.5$\,\AA\ cutoff, with
cubic-Hermite splines over $1.35$--$1.85$ and $4.5$--$5.5$\,\AA. This screening is the physics the
raw-Coulomb diagnostic (constant $\epsilon$; AUROC $0.549$) omitted; we hypothesized it would suppress
long-range charge noise and up-weight genuine salt bridges, but the offline gate below rejects that
hypothesis (AUROC $0.502$, chance). Note that $\epsilon$ depends on the pair \emph{distance} alone: as
in Rosetta REF2015 (Alford 2017, Eq.\ 7) and \ttk{tmol}'s \ttk{elec()}, burial is only \emph{approximated}
by distance (close $\Rightarrow$ low $\epsilon$), never measured --- there is no solvent-accessibility
or generalized-Born term. Charges $q_i$ are the net-correct \emph{formal} charged-group values
(heavy-atom-only complexes; the same \ttk{CHARGE} table as the diagnostic); the sigmoid constants are
Rosetta REF2015 defaults, and the NumPy port (\ttk{scripts/\_faelec.py}) is validated byte-for-byte
against compiled \ttk{tmol} \ttk{elec()}. Reward $\R_{\mathrm{elec}}=-E_{\mathrm{elec}}$; constants at
right.}

\paragraph{Offline gate for $\R_{\mathrm{elec}}$: the screening does not rescue it (NEGATIVE).} We ran
the byte-faithful \ttk{fa\_elec} through the same discrimination gate as \ttk{fa\_sol}, on the identical
high--success--rate Complexa pack ($3439$ LigandMPNN-packed designs, $28.7\%$ Protenix-pass, $35$
targets). The hypothesis fails: screened \ttk{e\_elec} scores AUROC \textbf{$0.502$ --- pure chance},
\emph{below} even the raw constant-dielectric Coulomb diagnostic it was meant to fix ($0.549$, Table
\ref{tab:potentials}) and far below $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$'s $0.720$ on this pool. Every diagnostic agrees
it carries no design-level signal: the size-controlled logit coefficient is
$\beta(\cdot\,|\,\text{len},\text{ifsz})={+}0.040$ (negligible, and the \emph{wrong} sign for a
favorable-energy term), and the pass rate is flat across \ttk{e\_elec} quartiles
($30.7,\,30.8,\,21.8,\,30.2\%$ --- no monotonic trend). Making the dielectric physically correct did
not help; if anything it \emph{removed} the weak size-correlation that gave the crude $1/d^2$ version
its (already near-dead) $0.549$. Two mechanisms explain the null result, both consequences of choices
forced by the packed-complex representation. \emph{(i) The dielectric is burial-blind.} As emphasized
above, $\epsilon$ is a function of pair distance only; it approximates burial by the crude proxy
``close $\Rightarrow$ buried,'' which is precisely the assumption that breaks at a designed
protein--protein interface, where many close charge pairs are solvent-exposed and the genuinely buried
salt bridges the reward should reward are not systematically the closest ones. \emph{(ii) Formal,
heavy-atom-only charges.} Because hydrogens are stripped, we use formal charged-group charges
(Asp/Glu/Lys/Arg/His) and see none of the backbone and polar side-chain partial-charge dipoles that
carry much of the real electrostatic complementarity. \textbf{\ttk{fa\_elec} does not earn a GRPO run
on this stack}; a burial-aware dielectric (SASA-scaled or generalized-Born) or a full partial-charge
model on protonated complexes would be prerequisites to revisiting it, and even then it would be gated
before any reward use. This is the same discipline that flagged \ttk{fa\_sol} before training and was
then vindicated at step $300$ (Table \ref{tab:fasoleval}): a term that cannot separate pass from fail
\emph{even on high-quality designs} has no business being a reward.

\subsection{Combining the two chemistry terms: a physically-weighted \texorpdfstring{$\R_{\mathrm{sol}}+\R_{\mathrm{elec}}$}{fa\_sol + fa\_elec} reward}
\label{sec:solelec-combined}
Individually the two chemistry terms fail for \emph{complementary} reasons
(Table \ref{tab:chemeval}), and those reasons are
exactly why their \emph{sum} is worth a look. \ttk{fa\_sol} carries genuine per-residue signal
($+0.20$, Table \ref{tab:fasolnorm}) but the step-$300$ GRPO run hacked it by the trivial route
(Table \ref{tab:fasoleval}): desolvation is minimized by \emph{removing} buried polar atoms
(\ttk{fa\_sol\_per\_atom} $0.143\!\to\!0.085$, electrostatics \ttk{e\_elec} $-3.30\!\to\!-0.70$), i.e.\
by building a smaller, weaker, all-hydrophobic interface. \ttk{fa\_elec} alone is burial-blind chance
($0.502$). But the physics ties them together: burying an unsatisfied polar atom is a \ttk{fa\_sol}
\emph{penalty} that is \emph{worth paying only if a salt bridge or H-bond repays it}
(\S\ref{sec:biochem-complementarity}, and the \ttk{fa\_sol} term itself credits no electrostatics).
A reward that sees only \ttk{fa\_sol} rewards the hack of never paying the cost; a reward that sees the
\emph{net} \ttk{fa\_sol}$+$\ttk{fa\_elec} energy can only lower it by paying the desolvation cost
\emph{and} collecting the compensating electrostatic credit --- precisely the buried, satisfied salt
bridge REF2015 rewards. Figure \ref{fig:solelec} shows this coupling directly: the combined term is
favorable only for a satisfied salt bridge (opposite-charged), and correctly penalizes both
same-charged repulsion and a lone buried charge with no electrostatic partner. This is the two-term
restriction of the interface complementarity energy of
Eq.\ \ref{eq:eiface}:
\begin{equation}
E_{\mathrm{sol+elec}}=\!\!\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal I}\!\big[\,w_{\mathrm{sol}}\,E^{ij}_{\mathrm{sol}}
  +w_{\mathrm{elec}}\,E^{ij}_{\mathrm{elec}}\big],
\qquad
\R_{\mathrm{sol+elec}}=-\,\frac{E_{\mathrm{sol+elec}}}{N_{\mathrm{iface}}},
\label{eq:solelec}
\end{equation}
with the \textbf{REF2015 default weights} $w_{\mathrm{sol}}{=}1.0$ and $w_{\mathrm{elec}}{=}1.0$
(Alford et al.\ 2017 \cite{alford2017}, Table 1; both terms already in kcal/mol, so the combination is
a straight sum). \ttk{tmol} ships no \ttk{ref2015.wts} file --- its per-term weights live in the score
graph configuration --- so we take the canonical published REF2015 values, the same $1.0/1.0$ the
composite of Eq.\ \ref{eq:eiface} already uses.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.98\linewidth]{figs/solelec_combined.png}
\caption{\textbf{The combined term $E=$\ttk{fa\_sol}$+$\ttk{fa\_elec} is well-defined exactly where
the standalone terms are not: it scores interface chemistry by \emph{pair identity}.} Both panels are
computed from the byte-faithful ports the reward itself calls (\ttk{scripts/\_faelec.py} \ttk{elec\_pair},
\ttk{scripts/\_fasol.py} \ttk{fa\_sol\_pairs}; the same sigmoidal-DDD electrostatics and LK desolvation
as the \ttk{w\_solelec\_scalar} arm, each validated max$|\Delta|<10^{-6}$ vs compiled \ttk{tmol}), over
heavy-atom separation $d$ with the reward's formal side-chain charges (Asp~O~${-}0.5$, Lys~N~${+}1.0$).
\textbf{(a)}~For a carboxylate--ammonium salt bridge, \ttk{fa\_sol} is a desolvation \emph{penalty}
($+$, burying two polar groups) while \ttk{fa\_elec} is an attractive \emph{credit} ($-$); their
physical-unit sum $E$ (purple, Eq.\ \ref{eq:solelec}) is net favorable --- the electrostatic credit
more than repays the desolvation cost ($E{=}{-}1.43$ at $d{=}3$\,\AA). \emph{This coupling is the whole
point}: the reward can only fall by paying desolvation \emph{and} collecting the compensating salt
bridge, so it cannot be gamed by the retraction hack that collapsed standalone \ttk{fa\_sol}.
\textbf{(b)}~The aggregate $E(d)$ separates cleanly by pair chemistry (values at $d{=}3$\,\AA):
\emph{opposite-charged} Asp$\cdots$Lys dips favorable ($E{=}{-}1.43$; credit repays cost);
\emph{same-charged} Lys$\cdots$Lys is strongly penalized ($E{=}{+}5.92$; electrostatic repulsion
\emph{adds} to desolvation); \emph{aliphatic} Leu$\cdots$Val is near-neutral, mildly favorable
($E{=}{-}0.39$; cheap nonpolar burial, no charge); and \emph{aliphatic${+}$charged} Leu$\cdots$Lys pays
a lone desolvation cost with no electrostatic return ($E{=}{+}0.63$; a buried unsatisfied charge).
This is the discrimination the independently-$z$-scored standalone \ttk{fa\_elec} arm lacked (offline
AUROC $0.502\approx$ chance; its training-time \ttk{elec\_scalar} never fell, Sec.\ \ref{sec:solelec-combined}):
combining in physical units before a \emph{single} $z$-score lets the small but sign-informative
electrostatic term ride the larger, coherent desolvation magnitude instead of being normalized into
noise.}
\label{fig:solelec}
\end{figure}

\caption{\textbf{Adding either chemistry term hurts, and the two fail for complementary reasons.}
Table-6 headline metrics (cold-A8 sampler, $380$ designs, target-balanced macro) for the
interface-chemistry GRPO arms against the pre-GRPO base and the no-chemistry sparse $e_{lj}{+}R_g$
reference. The no-chem sparse $e_{lj}{+}R_g$ arm \emph{(s300)} is the strongest $e_{lj}$-family arm
to date (pass\% $3.95$, Table \ref{tab:eljrog}); \emph{layering \ttk{fa\_sol} on that same base
collapses it to $1.32$} (this fa\_sol \emph{(s300)} row is the arm dissected in
Table \ref{tab:fasoleval}, $w_{\mathrm{fasol}}{=}0.5$), and on the stronger $8$-term base both
\ttk{fa\_sol} \emph{(s800)} and \ttk{fa\_elec} \emph{(s750)} ($w{=}0.5$) land at $1.84$ --- still a
third below base's $5.61$ --- while \ttk{fa\_sol} \emph{erodes further} to $0.79$ by step $1450$
\emph{even as} its reward proxies all keep improving ($e_{lj}\,1.13\!\to\!{-}8.01$, $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$,
SC and ddG all best-in-table at s1450): the same learn-then-diverge reward-hacking split as
Table \ref{tab:eljrogtraj}, where the checkpoint that scores its own reward best designs worst.
\textbf{No chemistry arm beats the no-chem reference, let alone the base} --- including the
physically-weighted \emph{combined} \ttk{fa\_sol}${+}$\ttk{fa\_elec} arm (Eq.\ \ref{eq:solelec},
blue rows), which at \emph{s300} lands at pass\% $1.84$, identical to the two standalone chemistry arms
and still a third below the no-chem $3.95$; coupling desolvation to its electrostatic repayment did
not, at least by step $300$, convert to pass\%, consistent with the combined score's offline-gate
AUROC ($0.549$, below \ttk{fa\_sol}'s $0.594$). Continuing the arm to \emph{s525} \emph{erodes} it
further to pass\% $1.58$ even as both reward energies keep improving (\ttk{fa\_sol}
$50.0\!\to\!42.8$, $e_{lj}\,15.04\!\to\!13.66$) --- the same learn-then-diverge split as the
\ttk{fa\_sol} arm above. Notably the combined arm reaches its
\ttk{fa\_elec} ${-}2.46$ / \ttk{fa\_sol} $50.0$ with a bounded repulsive cost (\ttk{fa\_rep} $81.0$)
between the no-chem ($74.8$) and \ttk{fa\_elec}-only ($103.7$) arms --- it collects more electrostatic
credit than \ttk{fa\_sol}-only ($-0.70$) without \ttk{fa\_elec}-only's blown-up clash bill, yet the
cleaner energetics still do not lift pass\%.
The complementarity is the point: \ttk{fa\_sol} keeps the interface \emph{physically clean} (bounded
$e_{lj}\,0.52$/$1.13$, low $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$, high SC) but hacks its reward by \emph{retracting}
the interface (iface f below the native $0.14$--$0.16$ band, $R_g$ bloated), whereas \ttk{fa\_elec}
chases electrostatics-adjacent quality (best $H$, 3Di TV, and ddG) at the cost of a \emph{blown-up}
$e_{lj}\,22.87$ --- it credits salt bridges while ignoring the steric clashes that pay for them. Each
term's failure is the other term's strength, which is exactly why the physically-weighted \emph{sum}
(Eq.\ \ref{eq:solelec}) --- net energy can only fall by paying desolvation \emph{and} collecting the
compensating electrostatic credit --- is the principled fix. The two reward quantities themselves
(columns \ttk{fa\_sol}, \ttk{fa\_elec}; interface means over the $380$ packed designs,
\ttk{scripts/\_arm\_reward\_report.py}) make the tradeoff literal: \ttk{fa\_sol} drives desolvation down
(\ttk{fa\_sol} $26.2$ at both s300 and s1450) but collects almost no electrostatic credit
(\ttk{fa\_elec} ${\approx}{-}0.7$), whereas \ttk{fa\_elec} earns more electrostatic credit
(\ttk{fa\_elec} ${-}1.38$) only by paying a far larger desolvation penalty (\ttk{fa\_sol} $56.3$) ---
and \emph{neither chemistry arm matches the no-chem arm's electrostatics} (\ttk{fa\_elec} ${-}3.30$),
which builds a genuinely better-satisfied interface without optimizing either term directly. Each arm
minimizes the energy it is shown and lets the coupled one drift --- the failure mode the combined
reward closes. The size-normalized H-bond density (\ttk{n\_hb}$/$res, H-bonds per interface residue)
tells the same story from the polar-contact side: the \ttk{fa\_sol} arms build the \emph{fewest}
interface H-bonds per residue ($0.088$--$0.105$, the desolvation hack literally avoids burying polar
groups), whereas \ttk{fa\_elec} ($0.183$) and the no-chem reference ($0.185$) build twice the density
--- the electrostatic term earns its salt-bridge/H-bond credit, but at the $e_{lj}\,22.87$ clash cost
above. (\ttk{fa\_elec} is the byte-faithful Rosetta REF2015 electrostatics term, \ttk{scripts/\_faelec.py}
validated max$|\Delta|<10^{-6}$ vs compiled \ttk{tmol}; it was previously reported under the
\ttk{e\_elec} column name.) \textbf{Bold} $=$ best \emph{trained} arm
per column (by header direction; iface f and $R_g$ scored by closeness to native). Base
$e_{lj}$/ipSAE pending the base-A8 pack.}
\label{tab:chemeval}
\end{table}

\paragraph{Why the weighting must be applied \emph{before} normalization.} The subtlety --- and the
reason the naive combination is wrong --- is \emph{where} the linear combination happens. The current
\ttk{fa\_sol} and \ttk{fa\_elec} companion arms each self-normalize independently: each energy is
divided by $N_{\mathrm{iface}}$, then \emph{separately} $z$-scored within the rollout group, and the two
$z$-scores are added with per-arm weights ($w_{\mathrm{fasol}},w_{\mathrm{elec}}$). Group $z$-scoring
rescales each term to unit variance, which \emph{discards its kcal/mol magnitude} --- so two separate
$z$-scores give \ttk{fa\_sol} and \ttk{fa\_elec} \emph{equal} influence on the advantage regardless of
their true energy scale, and the sum is an arbitrary equal-variance blend, \emph{not} the physical
REF2015 energy. (On heavy-atom-only formal-charge complexes \ttk{fa\_elec} is small in magnitude next
to \ttk{fa\_sol}; separate $z$-scoring silently up-weights it by an order of magnitude.) The
Rosetta-faithful reward instead forms the linear combination in physical units \emph{first}
(Eq.\ \ref{eq:solelec}: sum $w_{\mathrm{sol}}E_{\mathrm{sol}}+w_{\mathrm{elec}}E_{\mathrm{elec}}$ in
kcal/mol, normalize by interface size once, negate), and applies a \textbf{single} group $z$-score to
that one combined penalty. The physical $1{:}1$ weighting is thus preserved end-to-end; the lone
group-relative rescale is the same variance normalization every other scalar arm uses, applied once to
the composite rather than twice to its parts. Because both companion scalars are already the
per-interface-residue energies $E_{\mathrm{sol}}/N_{\mathrm{iface}}$ and
$E_{\mathrm{elec}}/N_{\mathrm{iface}}$ (divided by the \emph{same} $N_{\mathrm{iface}}$), the combined
per-residue penalty is simply their weighted sum
$w_{\mathrm{sol}}\,\ttk{fasol\_scalar}+w_{\mathrm{elec}}\,\ttk{elec\_scalar}$ --- no new pack, no new
worker output, reusing verbatim the byte-validated \ttk{fa\_sol} and \ttk{fa\_elec} the two arms
already compute on the shared LigandMPNN pack.

\paragraph{Wiring and gate-first discipline.} The reward is a single new scalar weight
$w_{\mathrm{solelec}}$ (config \ttk{w\_solelec\_scalar}) with two physical sub-weights defaulting to
the REF2015 $1.0/1.0$ (\ttk{w\_solelec\_wsol}, \ttk{w\_solelec\_welec}); it is mutually informative
with, but distinct from, the separate \ttk{w\_fasol\_scalar}/\ttk{w\_elec\_scalar} arms (one would run
the combined term \emph{instead of} the two separate ones, not on top). It rides the same ``pot'' want
on the $e_{lj}$ repack (no extra pack) and, like the $e_{lj}+R_g$ winner, adds
$w_{\mathrm{solelec}}\cdot z(\R_{\mathrm{sol+elec}})$ to the design-level advantage. Crucially, the
same discipline that flagged \ttk{fa\_sol} before training and rejected \ttk{fa\_elec} outright applies
here: \textbf{before any GRPO run}, the combined score
$\R_{\mathrm{sol+elec}}$ must clear the offline discrimination gate on the $3439$-design Complexa pack
(Table \ref{tab:potentials}'s protocol) --- the go/no-go is whether the \emph{physically-weighted sum}
separates Protenix-pass from fail better than either part alone ($0.594$ raw \ttk{fa\_sol}, $0.502$
\ttk{fa\_elec}) and, ideally, approaches $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$'s $0.720$. The hypothesis is that coupling
the desolvation cost to its electrostatic repayment closes the hack that sank the standalone
\ttk{fa\_sol} run; the gate tests it on high-quality designs before a single GPU-hour is spent, exactly
as \S\ref{sec:biochem-complementarity} insists.

\paragraph{Hydrogen bonds (phase 2).} Once \ttk{fa\_sol} and \ttk{fa\_elec} are gated, the
orientation-dependent hydrogen-bond term completes the composite of Eq.\ \ref{eq:eiface} (Kortemme,
Morozov \& Baker 2003 \cite{kortemme2003}; refit in O'Meara et al.\ 2015 \cite{omeara2015} and
adopted by REF2015). It scores donor-hydrogen/acceptor pairs on \emph{four} geometric degrees of
freedom rather than distance alone,
\[
E_{\mathrm{hb}}=\!\!\sum_{(H,A)}\!\! w_H w_A\,f\!\Big(
   E_{\mathrm{hb}}^{HA}(d_{HA})+E_{\mathrm{hb}}^{AHD}(\theta_{AHD})
  +E_{\mathrm{hb}}^{BAH}(\theta_{BAH})+E_{\mathrm{hb}}^{B2BAH}(\phi_{B2BAH},\theta_{BAH})\Big),
\]
with $d_{HA}$ the H$\cdots$acceptor distance, $\theta_{AHD}$ the acceptor--H--donor angle,
$\theta_{BAH}$ the base--acceptor--H angle, $\phi_{B2BAH}$ the improper torsion about the acceptor,
$w_H,w_A$ atom-type weights, and $f$ a smoothing envelope over one-dimensional polynomial splines fit
to the $\sim$8{,}000-structure Top8000 set. Because a contact scores only when \emph{both} the
distance and the donor$\rightarrow$acceptor angles are near-ideal, it replaces the orientation-blind
H-bond count of Table \ref{tab:potentials}; it is deferred here purely on implementation cost
(donor/acceptor typing, hydrogen placement, angle/torsion measurement, and the fitted splines).

\centering

                    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{4.2cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{2.0cm}

                    >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{2.9cm}@{}}

\caption{\textbf{The per-pair interface-complementarity reward} $E_{\mathrm{iface}}$ (Eq.\
\ref{eq:eiface}), summed over cross-interface atom pairs at REF2015 default weights; each term is a
documented closed form we port to NumPy (or read from \ttk{tmol}) on the LigandMPNN pack. The vdW
term is already shipped as the bounded $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$. \textbf{For the initial experiments we
implement the two highlighted \textcolor{aTint}{\rule{1.6ex}{1.6ex}} terms --- \ttk{fa\_sol} and
dielectric-screened \ttk{fa\_elec}} (Figs.\ \ref{fig:fasol}--\ref{fig:faelec}), the chemistry $e_{lj}$
is blindest to and that need only distances and per-atom-type constants; the orientation-dependent
H-bond term is deferred to phase 2 (its four-DOF geometry and fitted splines are the costly part).
The statistical alternative (Miyazawa--Jernigan) is a cheaper residue-level lookup requiring only
C$\beta$ contacts --- usable as a backbone-only diagnostic or ablation, but orientation- and
desolvation-blind.}
\label{tab:biochem}

\paragraph{A cheaper knowledge-based alternative.} If we want a reward that needs \emph{no} pack ---
scoring the generated backbone directly --- the classical route is a knowledge-based residue--residue
statistical potential. The \textbf{Miyazawa--Jernigan} contact potential (1985 \cite{mj1985}; refined
1996 \cite{mj1996}) is a $20{\times}20$ matrix of effective inter-residue contact energies derived by
the quasi-chemical approximation from PDB contact statistics; the interface score is then a single
sum $\sum_{(p,q)\in\mathcal I_{\mathrm{res}}} e_{\mathrm{MJ}}(a_p,a_q)$ of matrix lookups over
contacting residue pairs (favorable for hydrophobic--hydrophobic burial, unfavorable for mismatched
pairs), needing only C$\beta$ positions. It is far cheaper than the atomistic score but coarser
(residue-level, orientation- and desolvation-blind), so we propose it as a backbone-only diagnostic /
ablation rather than the primary reward. (Distance-dependent statistical PMFs in the Sippl 1990
tradition are a middle ground but add per-pair distance histograms without capturing H-bond
orientation.)

\paragraph{Integration and validation.} Both initial terms reuse the interface-pair enumeration and
bounded-LJ machinery already in \ttk{scripts/\_packed\_potentials.py}: \ttk{fa\_sol} (Eq.\
\ref{eq:fasol}) and dielectric \ttk{fa\_elec} (Eq.\ \ref{eq:faelec}) are self-contained NumPy given
the Rosetta atom-type/charge tables, or read directly from \ttk{tmol}'s \ttk{beta\_nov2016} per-term
energies decomposed onto interface pairs --- \emph{no new geometry beyond the distances the LJ pass
already computes}. Following the discipline of Table \ref{tab:potentials}, we \textbf{gate before we
wire}: for the initial experiments, score \ttk{fa\_sol} and \ttk{fa\_elec} \emph{each alone} (and
their sum) on the same $3500$-design packed set against the Protenix pass label, and adopt each only
if it adds discrimination over the LJ-only $\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ (AUROC $0.778$) --- the same offline
gate that has killed every non-predictive candidate here. Whichever survives is added on top of the
winning sparse $e_{lj}$ arm as a single controlled variable, in the sparse-scalar delivery of
\S\ref{sec:elj-granularity} (or the exact per-residue split of \S\ref{sec:pertoken-pot}), with no new
train-time cost beyond the pack already paid for. The orientation-dependent H-bond term and the full
composite $E_{\mathrm{iface}}$ of Eq.\ \ref{eq:eiface} follow once \ttk{fa\_sol}/\ttk{fa\_elec} are
gated, with the REF2015 default weights as the starting point, recalibrated by the same
size-controlled logistic fit used per-term in Table \ref{tab:potentials}.

\section{The third reward set: Protenix fold-consistency distillation}
\label{sec:protenix-sft}
Every reward so far is computed on structure the policy \emph{itself} emitted --- the generated
backbone (\S3.2, \S3.5), its LigandMPNN pack (\S3.3, \S4), or its 3Di tokens (\S3.1). None of them
can tell the policy that the structure it drew is not the structure its own sequence \emph{folds
into}. The diagnostic that exposes this is the fold/dock split of \S3.4: refolding the step-50 arm
with Protenix reproduces the binder \emph{monomer} fold (binder-only C$\alpha$-RMSD median
$2.4$\,\AA) but disagrees on the \emph{docking pose} (antigen-aligned binder L-RMSD median
$29.5$\,\AA). The high ipTM is Protenix's confidence in \emph{an} interface, not the generated one.
No backbone-, pack-, or 3Di-histogram reward can move this, because none of them sees where the
sequence \emph{wants} to dock --- \textbf{docking-pose fidelity is the clearest open lever, and it is
a property of the sequence$\to$structure map, not of any quantity read off the generated pose.}

\textbf{The idea: the exact structural dual of CHORD (\S3.4).} CHORD distils the LigandMPNN
\emph{design} branch --- the sequence-given-structure conditional $q(a^{\ast}\!\mid\!X,T)$ --- into
the \ttk{sequence} track. Its mirror image is a \emph{fold} branch: run the policy's own output
sequence through Protenix (the same oracle that scores the benchmark) to predict the complex
structure $X^{\ast}\!\sim\!\mathcal P(a,T,E)$, then distil that structure into the two structural
tracks the policy denoises. This is the missing second arrow of the round-trip: \S2 added the
\emph{design} branch $\mathrm{MPNN}(X)\!\to\!a^{\ast}$ (sequence expert); we now add the \emph{fold}
branch $\mathrm{Protenix}(a)\!\to\!X^{\ast}$ (structure expert), whose fixed point is a policy whose
generated structure \emph{is} what its sequence folds and docks to.

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\small,>={Stealth[length=2.2mm]},
  core/.style={draw=lfLine,line width=1.1pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=2.6cm,minimum height=1.25cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=lfFill},
  mp/.style  ={draw=ioLine,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=2.9cm,minimum height=1.3cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=ioFill},
  ds/.style  ={draw=bLine,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=3.3cm,minimum height=1.35cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=bFill},
  fd/.style  ={draw=dLine,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=3.3cm,minimum height=1.35cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=dFill},
  rwB/.style ={draw=bLine,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=3.3cm,minimum height=1.5cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=bTint},
  rwD/.style ={draw=dLine,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=center,text width=3.3cm,minimum height=1.5cm,inner sep=3pt,fill=dTint},
  flow/.style={->,thick}]
\node[core] (lf)  at (0.0, 0.0) {\textbf{\textcolor{lfLine}{LeFlur} $\pi_\theta$}\\ backbone $X$,\\ seq $a$,\\ 3Di $\tau$};
\node[ds]   (ds)  at (4.3, 1.35){\textbf{\textcolor{bLine}{design}} $q(a^{\ast}\!\mid X,T)$\\ LigandMPNN\\ $\to$ seq expert $a^{\ast}$};
\node[fd]   (fd)  at (4.3,-1.35){\textbf{\textcolor{dLine}{fold}} $\mathcal P(X^{\ast}\!\mid a,T,E)$\\ Protenix\\ $\to$ struct expert $X^{\ast}$};
\node[rwB]  (rB)  at (9.0, 1.35){\textcolor{bLine}{CHORD seq SFT}\ (\S3.4)\\ distil $a^{\ast}\!\to$ \ttk{sequence}};
\node[rwD]  (rD)  at (9.0,-1.35){\textcolor{dLine}{Protenix struct SFT}\\ encode $X^{\ast}\!\to(s^{\ast},\tau^{\ast})$\\ distil into \ttk{structure}$+$\ttk{tri}};
\draw[flow,bLine] (lf.east) to[out=25,in=180]  (ds.west);
\draw[flow,dLine] (lf.east) to[out=-25,in=180] (fd.west);
\draw[flow,bLine] (ds) -- (rB);
\draw[flow,dLine] (fd) -- (rD);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

\textbf{Encoding the oracle structure into both structural tracks.} Protenix returns an all-atom
complex; we parse its binder chain to an atom14 backbone and run the \emph{same} encoders the policy
was trained against, so the expert targets live in exactly the policy's own vocabularies:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{LG / \ttk{structure\_tokens}} --- the backbone-geometry codec tokens $s^{\ast}$ (the
  fold), from the discrete local-geometry encoder;
\item \textbf{3Di / \ttk{tri\_tokens}} --- the 3Di structural-alphabet tokens $\tau^{\ast}$, from the
  3Di encoder (the \ttk{encode}-path tokenizer),
\end{itemize}
both defined per binder residue and aligned $1{:}1$ with the policy's generated positions. Deriving
\emph{both} tracks from the one Protenix structure (rather than only the fold, or only 3Di) is what
makes the target a self-consistent structure the policy can actually reach on both of its structural
heads.

\textbf{The distillation term (identical machinery to \S3.4).} Let $p^{s}_{s,l}=\pi_\theta(s^{\ast}_l
\!\mid\!x_{t_s})$ and $p^{\tau}_{s,l}=\pi_\theta(\tau^{\ast}_l\!\mid\!x_{t_s})$ be the policy's
structure- and tri-endpoint probabilities of the Protenix-derived expert token at supervised step
$s$, position $l$, evaluated --- as in CHORD --- on the policy's \emph{own} noised trajectory states
(DAgger: the fold oracle is queried on the learner's sequence). With the same $\phi$-weighting
$\phi=p(1-p)$ (detached) that zeroes consensus and rejected tokens, and the same masked-only,
whole-binder scope, the structure SFT loss is the token-CE toward the oracle tokens, summed over the
two structural tracks:
\[
\mathcal L_{\text{SFT-}\phi}^{\text{struct}}
   =\frac1{|S|}\sum_{s\in S}
     \Big[\,w_s\,\ell^{\,s}_s(s^{\ast})\;+\;w_\tau\,\ell^{\,\tau}_s(\tau^{\ast})\,\Big],
\qquad
\mathcal L=(1-\mu)\mathcal L_{\mathrm{GRPO}}+\mu_{\text{seq}}\mathcal L_{\text{SFT}}^{\text{seq}}
   +\mu_{\text{str}}\mathcal L_{\text{SFT-}\phi}^{\text{struct}},
\]
each $\ell_s$ the $\phi$-weighted CE of Eq.\ (\S3.4). Because the target is the expert
\emph{structure}, the natural context for the endpoint forward is the expert's own structural tokens
(the $s^{\ast},\tau^{\ast}$ of $X^{\ast}$) at revealed positions --- exactly the expert-context fix
that \S3.4 applies on the sequence side (\ttk{\_expert\_context\_seq}), here on the structure/tri
tracks --- so the distilled conditional is $p_\theta(X^{\ast}\!\mid\!X^{\ast}\text{-context},a,T)$,
whose fixed point is the Protenix fold-and-dock, not a policy/oracle chimera.

\textbf{Why not just the terminal scalar.} A scalar consistency reward (generated-vs-Protenix
complex RMSD, or a TM/lDDT agreement) is available and will be tracked, but --- as for AAR vs.\ CHORD
(\S3.4) --- a single number is a weak signal for a per-residue structure-prediction target and
credits every structure token equally. The dense form lands the gradient on the residues whose
generated geometry disagrees with the fold, which is where the docking-pose error concentrates.

\emph{Protenix is used here as a structure oracle, not a confidence reward.} Consistent with the
rest of the stack (all \ttk{iptm}/\ttk{ptm}/\ttk{plddt} terms are ungraded offline labels), we
distil Protenix's predicted \emph{coordinates} --- never its scalar confidence --- into the policy.

\subsection{Docking correctness: relabeled conditioning $+$ a dock reward}
\label{sec:dock}
The fold/dock split has a second consequence for the SFT above. Generation conditions on a specified
epitope $E$ (bidirectional hotspot conditioning $+$ epitope CFG, \S1), but Protenix routinely docks
the policy sequence at a \emph{different} site $E'\!\neq\!E$ (the $29.5$\,\AA\ antigen-aligned
L-RMSD; the high ipTM is confidence in \emph{an} interface, not the requested one). The structure SFT
as described conditions its endpoint forward on the originally-specified $E$ while the target
$X^{\ast}$ realizes $E'$ --- distilling the inconsistent conditional $p(\text{structure}@E'\mid
\text{cond}{=}E)$, the exact conditioning-side analogue of the sequence-context chimera fixed in
\S3.4. We fix it the same way, and add the missing on-target pressure as a separate scalar, both off
\emph{one} shared primitive so no extra fold is spent.

\textbf{Shared primitive: the achieved interface from $X^{\ast}$.} From the Protenix complex derive
the interface the sequence actually made, by heavy-atom contact (cutoff $\approx\!5$\,\AA):
$E'$ $=$ antigen residues contacting the binder, $P'$ $=$ its binder-side dual
(\ttk{derive\_interface\_from\_structure}, placed beside the $s^{\ast}/\tau^{\ast}$ encoders). Both
changes below read this one contact set.

\textbf{Relabel the SFT conditioning ($E\!\to\!E'$).} Rebuild the conditioning tensor from
$(E',P')$ --- the identical construction to the generation path --- and condition the structure-SFT
endpoint forward on it (a \ttk{conditioning\_override} on the endpoint call; the single edit point
that today reads \ttk{static["conditioning\_tensor"]} unchanged). The SFT then trains
$p_\theta(\text{structure}@E'\mid\text{cond}{=}E')$ --- a proper function of the conditioning that
generalizes back to the requested epitope at inference ($\text{cond}{=}E\!\to\!\text{structure}@E$).
Every design is kept (no dock-gate, no discarded data) and the target is always self-consistent. This
is \emph{coherence} supervision; it does not by itself make the policy dock where we asked.

\textbf{The dock reward $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ (scalar).} On-target docking is the orthogonal signal,
supplied by a scalar term on the same fold. For the specified epitope $E$, the $X^{\ast}$
contacted-antigen set $\mathcal C$, and $d_e$ the minimum distance from antigen residue $e$ to any
binder heavy atom,
\[
r_{\mathrm{rec}}=\frac1{|E|}\sum_{e\in E}\sigma\!\Big(\tfrac{d_0-d_e}{w}\Big),
\qquad
r_{\mathrm{prec}}=\frac{|\,\mathcal C\cap E\,|}{|\mathcal C|},
\qquad
\R_{\mathrm{dock}}=\frac{2\,r_{\mathrm{rec}}\,r_{\mathrm{prec}}}{r_{\mathrm{rec}}+r_{\mathrm{prec}}}
\in[0,1],
\]
with $d_0\!\approx\!5\text{--}8$\,\AA\ and $w\!\approx\!1\text{--}2$\,\AA. Soft recall rewards
\emph{actually touching} the requested residues (a dense gradient, not a $0/1$ indicator); soft
precision is the anti-smear guard --- it stops the policy from wrapping the whole antigen to trivially
cover $E$; their harmonic mean penalizes both misses and smearing. $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ enters the
GRPO objective as a standard group-relative (Dr.GRPO) scalar at weight $w_{\mathrm{dock}}$, alongside
clash/shape/3Di-TV. It is the on-target counterpart to ipTM: ipTM says ``there is a confident
interface,'' $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ says ``it is the one we asked for'' --- putting the epitope
objective in the \emph{gradient} rather than leaning on inference-time CFG. Pass
($\text{pTM}\!>\!0.8\wedge\text{ipTM}\!>\!0.7$) stays an offline label.

\textbf{Division of labor and guards.} The two signals are orthogonal: a coherent-but-off-target
design keeps full (relabeled) SFT credit yet earns low $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$; an
on-target-but-incoherent design earns high $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ yet low SFT --- together they pull
toward coherent \emph{and} on-target. Because the relabel lets the SFT happily teach coherent
\emph{off}-target folds, $w_{\mathrm{dock}}$ must be strong enough to counter it (too weak and the
policy drifts off-epitope). The precision term and the co-active clash/shape rewards guard the
interpenetration/smear failure mode of \S3.2; $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ may optionally be gated by a
minimum pTM so it never credits a garbage fold that happens to touch $E$. \emph{Caveat:} it trusts
Protenix's docked pose and inherits its pose noise, mitigated by multi-seed averaging. As with every
reward before it enters the gradient, $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ is first validated offline --- it must
separate passing-Complexa complexes from off-epitope generations.

\textbf{Cost and integration.} Unlike the first two reward sets, this one is \emph{not} amortized on
the free pack: it spends one Protenix fold per supervised design --- and the relabel and
$\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ both reuse \emph{that same} fold, at zero extra fold cost. The worker-pool $+$
client that already serve the offline label (\ttk{scripts/protenix\_reward\_server.py}) are reused;
the term is gated behind its own \ttk{N\_WORKERS}$>0$ and a subsample fraction (fold a fraction of the
group, cache by sequence) so the fold budget is a tunable knob rather than a per-rollout tax. It is
proposed as the third set precisely because it buys the one axis the Protenix-free sets cannot --- the
dock --- at a Protenix-in-the-loop price.

\emph{Implementation status.} The structure-SFT distillation term
($\mu_{\text{str}},w_s,w_\tau,\phi$, expert-context, masked-only) is code-complete and wired live in
the trainer, config-reachable, and covered by unit tests; the first fresh-\ttk{OUTBASE} run with
$\mu_{\text{str}}{>}0$ is being launched. The docking additions of this subsection --- the shared
$X^{\ast}$-interface primitive, the conditioning relabel, and $\R_{\mathrm{dock}}$ --- are fully
specified (\ttk{docs/leflur/grpo\_dock\_correctness\_reward\_plan.md}) and are the next increment,
not yet wired.

\section{From reward to gradient}
\label{sec:reward2grad}
All rewards act through GRPO on the \emph{same} stored rollout trajectory. Each inner PPO
iteration draws one step subset $S=\{s_1,\dots,s_k\}$, $k=\ttk{steps\_per\_update}=3$, uniformly
from the $40$ steps (\ttk{\_sample\_step\_subset}); both the policy log-prob and the CHORD CE are
evaluated on this \emph{same} $S$.

\textbf{Differentiable transition log-prob.} Every reward gradient flows through the probability the
policy assigned to the moves it actually sampled. We rebuild that probability from the stored head
logits differentiably (\ttk{dfm\_step\_logprob}), reproducing the absorbing-state discrete
flow-matching (DFM) sampler out-of-place so gradients reach the logits.

Fix one track and one denoising step, from time $t\in[0,1)$ to $t+\mathrm dt$ ($\mathrm dt$ the step
size). For position $l$ let $z_l$ be the model's logits over the vocabulary (predicting the
\emph{clean} token, mask column set to $-\infty$); $T$ the sampling temperature; $\eta$ the
stochasticity; $\mathbb 1^{\mathrm{msk}}_l=[x_{t,l}{=}\text{mask}]$ the indicator that $l$ is
currently masked; and $p^{(1)}_l=\mathrm{softmax}(z_l/T)$ the predicted clean-token distribution.
The unnormalized transition weights $\tilde q_l$ over the vocabulary have two parts --- \emph{unmask}
a masked position toward a clean token, or (before the final step) \emph{remask} an unmasked one:
\[
\tilde q_l \;=\;
\underbrace{\mathrm dt\,\frac{1+\eta t}{1-t}\,p^{(1)}_l\;\mathbb 1^{\mathrm{msk}}_l}_{\text{unmask}}
\;+\;
\underbrace{\mathrm dt\,\eta\,e_{\text{mask}}\,\big(1-\mathbb 1^{\mathrm{msk}}_l\big)\,\mathbb 1[t{+}\mathrm dt<1]}_{\text{remask}},
\]
where $e_{\text{mask}}$ is the one-hot vector on the mask token. Regularizing
($q_l=\mathrm{reg}(\tilde q_l)$: clamp to $[0,1]$, then place the leftover mass on the ``stay-put''
self-transition at $x_{t,l}$) makes each $q_l$ a proper transition distribution. The sampler draws
from the row-normalized $q_l$, so the log-probability of the sampled trajectory --- summed over the
step subset $S$, its tracks $\tau$, and the generated positions $\mathrm{gen}^\tau$ of each track
(fixed/inpainted positions excluded) --- is
\[
\log\pi_\theta(S)=\sum_{s\in S}\ \sum_{\tau}\ \sum_{l\in\mathrm{gen}^\tau}
   \log\frac{q_l\!\big(x_{t+\mathrm dt,\,l}\big)}{\sum_{v} q_l(v)} ,
\]
with $x_{t+\mathrm dt,l}$ the token actually sampled at that position and $v$ ranging over the
vocabulary. \emph{Two callers, one kernel:} GRPO evaluates it on the \emph{biased} logits the sampler
actually drew from (classifier-free guidance $+$ inference bias, via
\ttk{\_recompute\_biased\_step\_logits}); CHORD's SFT term uses the \emph{raw conditional}
sequence-endpoint logits (no CFG/bias, via \ttk{\_sft\_seq\_endpoint\_logits}).

\textbf{Scalar advantage and clipped surrogate.} A design's scalar reward $r_i$ (the weighted sum
of the three scalar terms above) becomes a group-relative advantage, and that one number
multiplies the design's entire summed log-prob:
\[
A_i=\frac{r_i-\bar r}{\sigma_r+\epsilon}\ \ (\ttk{normalize\_advantage}),
\qquad
\rho_i=\exp\!\big(\log\pi_\theta^{(i)}(S)-\log\pi_{\theta_{\mathrm{old}}}^{(i)}(S)\big),
\]
\[
\mathcal L_{\mathrm{GRPO}}=-\frac1G\sum_i\min\!\big(\rho_i A_i,\ \mathrm{clip}(\rho_i,1{\pm}\varepsilon)\,A_i\big),
\qquad G=\ttk{group\_size}=64 .
\]
The no-grad snapshot $\log\pi_{\theta_{\mathrm{old}}}$ gives $\rho_i\!\approx\!1$ at the first
inner iteration (\ttk{ppo/ratio\_init}). The two dense terms add to this: CHORD via the
$\mu$-blend, per-token clash via the added per-position surrogate.

\subsection{How the terms combine: the flat basket and the z-score arms}
\label{sec:reward-weighting}
The advantage $A_i$ above is written as if there were a single scalar reward $r_i$, but the live arms
build $A_i$ through \emph{two distinct weighting mechanisms}, and a term's weight means something
different in each. Getting this right is what tells you a nominal ``\ttk{weight}$=1.0$'' term is loud
or nearly silent. We use the ten-term arm
\ttk{...elj\_sparse\_clash\_chainbreak\_lc\_jaccard\_rog\_solelec\_esmfoldsctm} (nine inherited terms
plus the ESMFold binder-monomer sc-TM term) as the running example.

\textbf{Mechanism A --- the flat basket (one shared normalization).} Most terms are summed into a
single per-design scalar and then \emph{one} group normalization ($\ttk{normalize\_advantage}=$ true)
rescales the whole sum:
\[
R_i=\sum_{k\in\mathcal F} w_k\,\R_k(i),
\qquad
A^{\flat}_i=\frac{R_i-\overline{R}_g}{\sigma_g(R)+\epsilon},
\qquad \mathcal F=\{\text{LC seq, LC 3Di, Jaccard seq/3Di, clash}{+}\text{contact, chainbreak, }R_g\text{, sc-TM}\}.
\]
Here $w_k$ is applied \emph{before} the sum, so it sets a term's size only \emph{relative to its
basket-mates}; after summing, the single division by $\sigma_g(R)$ pins the whole basket to
$\sigma\!\approx\!1$. The real influence of a flat term is therefore not its weight but its
\emph{variance share} of the basket,
\[
\mathrm{share}_k=\frac{\operatorname{cov}_g\!\big(w_k\,\R_k,\,R\big)}{\operatorname{var}_g(R)},
\qquad \sum_{k\in\mathcal F}\mathrm{share}_k=1,
\]
so a \emph{saturated} term (near-constant across the group, hence tiny variance) contributes
$\approx\!0$ \emph{even at weight $1.0$}, while a \emph{high-variance} term captures a large share of
the one $\sigma$-unit at the same weight.

\textbf{Mechanism B --- the z-score arms (each its own $\sigma$-unit, added on top).} The two
interface-energy scalars do \emph{not} join the basket. Each is group-standardized on its own and
then added to the advantage:
\[
z(x)_i=\frac{x_i-\overline{x}_g}{\sigma_g(x)}\ \ (\text{NaN}\!\to\!0),
\qquad
A_i=A^{\flat}_i+\underbrace{\ttk{w\_elj\_scalar}\,z(-e_{lj})_i}_{\text{arm}}
        +\underbrace{\ttk{w\_solelec\_scalar}\,z\!\big(-(\R_{\mathrm{sol}}{+}\R_{\mathrm{elec}})\big)_i}_{\text{arm}}.
\]
Because each metric is standardized to $\sigma=1$ \emph{by itself} and then scaled by its weight, in
Mechanism B \textbf{the weight \emph{is} the contribution magnitude in $\sigma$ units}, independent of
every other term. With both weights $1.0$, $e_{lj}$ contributes exactly $\sigma\!\approx\!1$ and
sol$+$elec another $\sigma\!\approx\!1$ (the combined energy uses physical sub-weights
$\ttk{w\_solelec\_wsol}=\ttk{w\_solelec\_welec}=1.0$ \emph{inside} the z-score; see
\S\ref{sec:solelec-combined}, Eq.~\ref{eq:solelec}).

\textbf{The consequence.} $e_{lj}$ alone, sol$+$elec alone, \emph{and the entire eight-term flat
basket} each carry $\sigma\!\approx\!1$. The two energies get private $\sigma$-units; the eight flat
terms \emph{share} one $\sigma$-unit, split by variance (Fig.~\ref{fig:reward-weighting}).
