saturated ($\approx\!$constant across the group), \textcolor{aLine}{teal} moderate,
\textcolor{dLine}{blue} the bimodal sc-TM. \emph{Middle:} the two interface energies routed through
z-arms. \emph{Bottom (derived):} the flat sum $R_i$; its single group normalization $A^{\flat}_i$
($\sigma\!=\!1$ for the whole basket); each energy z-arm ($\sigma\!=\!1$ each, added on top); and the
total advantage $A_i$ that multiplies the design's summed log-prob.}
\label{tab:weightingexample}
\end{table}

\textbf{Per-term contribution.} Two decompositions of that advantage make the asymmetry concrete
(Table~\ref{tab:weightingshare}). \emph{Between blocks}, each energy arm carries $\sigma\!=\!1$ and the
\emph{whole} eight-term basket carries $\sigma\!=\!1$ --- so on this arm the two energies together are
$\approx\!2/3$ of the advantage variance, and $e_{lj}$ \emph{alone} equals every flat term put
together. \emph{Inside the basket}, that one $\sigma$-unit is captured almost entirely by the two
high-variance terms: sc-TM $\approx\!61\%$ and clash$+$contact $\approx\!26\%$, with $R_g$ at
$\approx\!10\%$ and the four saturated terms (LC seq, LC 3Di, chainbreak, the Jaccards) together
$\approx\!3\%$ --- three of them at the \emph{same} nominal weight $1.0$ as sc-TM yet $\approx\!50\times$
quieter, purely because they barely vary across the group.

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering
{\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}%
\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{5.6cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{3.0cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{3.6cm}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{contributor} & \textbf{weight} & \textbf{effective influence}\\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{between blocks} (each an independent $\sigma$-unit)}\\
$e_{lj}$ arm                                   & $1.0$ & $\sigma=1.00$\\
$\R_{\mathrm{sol}}{+}\R_{\mathrm{elec}}$ arm   & $1.0$ & $\sigma=1.00$\\
entire flat basket (8 terms)                   & ---   & $\sigma=1.00$ \emph{(shared)}\\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{inside the flat basket} (variance shares, sum $=100\%$)}\\
\rowcolor{dTint}sc-TM               & $1.0$ & $\approx\!61\%$\\
\rowcolor{aTint}clash$+$contact     & $1.0$ & $\approx\!26\%$\\
$R_g$ compactness                   & $1.0$ & $\approx\!10\%$\\
Jaccard seq                         & $0.1$ & $\approx\!1.5\%$\\
\rowcolor{bTint}chainbreak          & $1.0$ & $\approx\!1.5\%$\\
Jaccard 3Di                         & $0.1$ & $\approx\!0.8\%$\\
\rowcolor{bTint}LC seq              & $1.0$ & $\approx\!-0.8\%$\\
\rowcolor{bTint}LC 3Di              & $1.0$ & $\approx\!-0.8\%$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\caption{Effective influence of each contributor in the $G=4$ example. \emph{Between blocks}: the two
z-arms and the entire flat basket each carry $\sigma\!\approx\!1$, so an energy arm equals the whole
basket. \emph{Inside the basket}: variance shares $\mathrm{share}_k=\operatorname{cov}_g(w_k\R_k,R)/\operatorname{var}_g(R)$
(they sum to $100\%$); the two high-variance terms (\textcolor{dLine}{sc-TM}, \textcolor{aLine}{clash})
take $\approx\!87\%$ while the \textcolor{bLine}{saturated} weight-$1.0$ terms take $\approx\!0\%$
(LC's tiny negative share is sampling noise about zero). The stand-alone $\sigma$ magnitudes do not add
in quadrature to the basket's $\sigma\!=\!1$ because the components are correlated across the shared
designs; the signed variance shares are the additive decomposition.}
\label{tab:weightingshare}
\end{table}

\textbf{Where sc-TM lands, and the design knob.} At \ttk{w\_sctm\_esmfold}$=1.0$ the ESMFold
binder-monomer sc-TM enters as one \emph{flat} voice. On our pool it is the \emph{loudest} flat term
(its broken-tail/consistent-chunk bimodality gives it a large variance share), but it still sits below
either energy arm: the effective ordering on this arm is
$e_{lj}\,(1.0)\approx\,\text{sol}{+}\text{elec}\,(1.0) > \text{sc-TM}\,(0.61) > \text{clash}\,(0.31) >
R_g\,(0.14) \gg \text{LC/chainbreak/Jaccard}\,(\sim\!0.01)$. To make sc-TM compete head-to-head with
$e_{lj}$ it would have to be \emph{promoted to its own z-arm} (a private $\sigma$-unit) rather than
left in the shared basket --- a deliberate change, not a weight tweak, since inside the basket even a
much larger flat weight is still capped by the single shared normalization.

\subsection{Update schematic}
The GRPO log-prob path (teal) and the CHORD SFT path (amber) fork off the \emph{same} shared step
subset $S$ and rejoin in the convex-blend loss. (Shown as two branches for clarity; the fast path
fuses them into one forward.)

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\footnotesize,>={Stealth[length=2mm]},
  data/.style={draw=ioLine,fill=ioFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.6cm},
  grpo/.style={draw=aLine,fill=aFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=6.0cm},
  sft/.style ={draw=bLine,fill=bFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=6.0cm},
  comb/.style={draw=lfLine,fill=lfFill,line width=1.0pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.6cm},
  fl/.style={->,thick,ioLine},flA/.style={->,thick,aLine},flB/.style={->,thick,bLine}]

\node[data] (roll) at (3.5, 0)
  {\textbf{rollout}\quad\ttk{generate\_sample(..., trajectory\_store)}\\[1pt]
   \ttk{trajectory["steps"]}\,$=[\,s_0\ s_1\ s_2\ \dots\ s_{39}\,]$\hfill\ttk{rollout\_nsteps}$=40$};
\node[data] (sub) at (3.5,-1.9)
  {\ttk{\_sample\_step\_subset(40)},\quad$k=\ttk{steps\_per\_update}=3$\\[1pt]
   $S=\{7,22,31\}$\ \ \textemdash\ ONE draw per inner iter, \textbf{SHARED}};

\node[grpo] (lp) at (0,-3.95)
  {\ttk{logprob\_over\_trajectory}$(S)$\\[1pt]\textcolor{aLine}{\itshape GRPO policy log-prob}};
\node[sft]  (sf) at (7,-3.95)
  {\ttk{sequence\_sft\_loss}$(S)$\\[1pt]\textcolor{bLine}{\itshape CHORD $\phi$-weighted CE}};

\node[grpo] (lpc) at (0,-6.35)
  {$\forall s\in S$:\ \ttk{\_recompute\_biased\_step\_logits}\\ \hfill(biased: CFG $+$ inference bias)\\[1pt]
   $\to$ \ttk{dfm\_step\_logprob}$(x_t\!\to\!x_{t+1})$\ \ $\Rightarrow$\ new\_lp $(B,)$};
\node[sft]  (sfc) at (7,-6.35)
  {$\forall s\in S$:\ \ttk{\_sft\_seq\_endpoint\_logits}\\ \hfill(UNbiased: no CFG / no bias)\\[1pt]
   $\to$ logits $(B,L,V{=}33)$};

\node[grpo] (lpr) at (0,-8.85)
  {$\rho=\exp(\text{new\_lp}-\text{old\_lp})$\\[1pt]
   $\mathcal L_{\mathrm{GRPO}}=-\min(\rho A,\,\mathrm{clip}(\rho)A).\mathrm{mean}()$};
\node[sft]  (sfr) at (7,-8.85)
  {$m=\mathrm{sup}\wedge\mathrm{gen\_mask\_seq}\wedge(x_t{=}\text{mask})$\\[1pt]
   $\phi=p_t(1-p_t)$ detached;\ $\mathrm{CE}=-\ln p_t$\\[1pt]
   $\ell_s=\sum(\mathrm{CE}\!\cdot\!\phi)/\sum\phi$;\ \ sft\_ce $=\overline{\ell_s}$ over $|S|$};

\node[comb] (loss) at (3.5,-10.9)
  {$\mathcal L=(1-\mu)\,\mathcal L_{\mathrm{GRPO}}+\mu\,\text{sft\_ce}+\beta\,\mathrm{KL}$
   \hfill$(\mu=\ttk{sft\_mu}=0.1,\ \beta=0)$};
\node[data] (bwd) at (3.5,-12.4)
  {$(\mathcal L/n_{\mathrm{live}}).\ttk{backward()}$\ \ accumulate over \ttk{accum\_targets}$=10$,\\[1pt]
   one \ttk{optimizer.step()} per inner iteration\ ($\ttk{mu}=2$ inner iters / rollout)};

\draw[fl] (roll) -- (sub);
\draw[flA] (sub.south) -- (lp.north);
\draw[flB] (sub.south) -- (sf.north);
\draw[flA] (lp) -- (lpc);  \draw[flB] (sf) -- (sfc);
\draw[flA] (lpc) -- (lpr); \draw[flB] (sfc) -- (sfr);
\draw[flA] (lpr.south) -- (loss.north);
\draw[flB] (sfr.south) -- (loss.north);
\draw[fl] (loss) -- (bwd);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

When \ttk{per\_token\_clash} is on, the \ttk{struct} track is removed from the left (GRPO) box and
gets its own per-position surrogate $\mathcal L^{\text{struct}}$ (\S3.5), added into
$\mathcal L_{\mathrm{GRPO}}$ before the blend; \ttk{seq}$+$\ttk{tri} keep the scalar-advantage
path shown.

\section{Live training configuration}
Two arms are live, and they are a deliberate \textbf{A/B on the fold-confidence reward source} over
an otherwise identical setup. Both share the same Dr.GRPO update
(\ttk{normalize\_advantage}$=$true, per-group $/\sigma$), warm-start from the combined sol$+$elec
arm's \ttk{grpo\_step\_525} checkpoint (itself descended from \ttk{leflur-binder-3di}), $G{=}64$,
\ttk{rollout\_nsteps}$=40$, \ttk{steps\_per\_update}$=3$, \ttk{mu}$=2$ inner iters,
\ttk{accum\_targets}$=10$ with \ttk{shuffle\_targets}$=$true (gradient averaged over 10 targets/step),
lr $10^{-5}$, $\beta{=}0$ (KL off), \ttk{eps\_clip}$=0.2$, \ttk{grad\_clip}$=1.0$, gradient
checkpointing on, epitope conditioning on, \ttk{sequence\_diversity\_penalty}$=2$,
\ttk{tri\_diversity\_penalty}$=8$. \emph{These last two are \textbf{sampler}-side per-design frequency
penalties applied during rollout generation, not reward terms.} Both CHORD sequence-SFT and Protenix
struct-SFT are off here (\ttk{sft\_mu}$=$\ttk{struct\_sft\_mu}$=0$), unlike the earlier CHORD runs.

Both arms also share an identical reward base: the isolated per-design z-score add-ons
\ttk{w\_elj\_scalar}$=1$ and the combined desolvation$+$electrostatics \ttk{w\_solelec\_scalar}$=1$
(physical sub-weights \ttk{w\_solelec\_wsol}$=$\ttk{w\_solelec\_welec}$=1.0$, Eq.~\ref{eq:solelec}),
plus the flat base-reward terms \ttk{w\_chainbreak}$=1$, \ttk{w\_clash\_contact}$=1$, \ttk{w\_rog}$=1$,
the anti-degeneracy \ttk{w\_seq\_complexity}$=1$ and \ttk{w\_struct\_complexity}$=1$, and --- now
\emph{graded}, unlike the runs to date where these were $0$ --- the reward-side diversity
\ttk{w\_seq\_diversity}$=$\ttk{w\_struct\_diversity}$=0.1$ (the proposed \ttk{w\_seq\_hamming} is still
$0$). The collapse of \S\ref{sec:seqcomplex} is what those two nonzero reward-side terms are meant to
address; note \emph{between}-design novelty (\ttk{diversity/seq\_novelty\_mean}) stays blind to it
(below).

The \textbf{only} difference between the two arms is the fold-confidence source.
\emph{Arm 1} (\ttk{\ldots\_solelec\_bindcraftpae\_zscore}) folds every rollout group with \textbf{BindCraft
AF2} ($24$ single-GPU workers, \ttk{FOLD\_BACKEND}$=$bindcraft, \ttk{need\_conf} on) and rewards the
interface PAE as an isolated per-design z-score add-on via \ttk{w\_pae\_interface\_scalar}$=1$ (detailed next). \emph{Arm 2}
(\ttk{\ldots\_solelec\_esmfoldsctm\_zscore}) uses \emph{no} AF2/Protenix fold pool at all --- every
AF2/Protenix confidence weight is $0$ and \ttk{need\_conf} is off --- and instead rewards
\textbf{ESMFold} binder-monomer self-consistency sc-TM as an isolated per-design z-score
\ttk{w\_sctm\_esmfold\_scalar}$=1$ (\S\ref{sec:reward-weighting}); that sc-TM is produced by the same
repack/shape pool that already computes $e_{lj}$ and sol$+$elec, so Arm 2 needs no dedicated fold
workers.

\paragraph{Arm 2 result (ESMFold sc-TM z-score): the reward is optimized, yet pass falls.} Evaluated at
training step $150$ through the identical cold A8 sampler and offline scorers as
Table~\ref{tab:seqablation} ($10$ designs $\times\,38$ targets $=380$), the ESMFold sc-TM z-score arm
\emph{cleans backbone geometry and pulls the whole-binder 3Di distribution toward Complexa} but
\emph{erodes sequence recovery and the Protenix pass rate} (Table~\ref{tab:esmfoldsctm}). Every geometry
diagnostic improves relative to the pre-GRPO base --- $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$ $24.7\!\to\!10.0$, break\%
$28.1\!\to\!19.5$, 3Di TV $0.437\!\to\!0.286$, Rg $16.9\!\to\!15.3$\,\AA{} and all-atom SC
$0.906\!\to\!0.919$ --- while pass\% $5.61\!\to\!3.95$, AAR $0.255\!\to\!0.230$, interface AAR
$0.222\!\to\!0.186$ and $C_{\mathrm{mpnn}}$ $0.108\!\to\!0.090$ all fall, the per-design 3Di linguistic
complexity collapses ($\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{3Di}}$ $0.267\!\to\!0.130$), interface $\Delta\Delta G$
worsens ($-37.9\!\to\!-32.3$) and ipSAE sits at its pool floor ($0.059$). This is the
geometry-cleaning-without-docking signature the offline gate predicted: ESMFold binder-monomer sc-TM is
docking-blind (it scores the binder folded \emph{alone}, never the complex).

(highest $H$, lowest $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$, best SC, and the highest generated-vs-predicted binder
TM in Table~\ref{tab:sctmselfconsist}) yet the \emph{docked complex} still disagrees with the fold
and ipSAE stays at floor --- the reward optimizes a docking-blind target. The last two columns are
the generated-vs-Protenix-predicted self-consistency TM-scores
(\ttk{scripts/\_grpo\_sctm\_metrics.py}, the same metric as Table~\ref{tab:sctmselfconsist}):
\textbf{TM bind} superposes the binder monomer alone, \textbf{TM cplx} the whole antigen+binder
complex (antigen-anchored, so docking-aware). They make the docking-blindness explicit ---
\textbf{TM bind} climbs steeply with the reward ($0.63\!\to\!0.78$) while \textbf{TM cplx} barely
moves ($0.56\!\to\!0.58$): the sequence increasingly re-folds to the generated binder \emph{shape}
but not to the generated \emph{pose}. The base row's TM cells are $-$ (its generated/Protenix pool
is not retained in the sc-TM layout).}
\label{tab:esmfoldsctm}

\paragraph{sc-TM before$\to$after (the optimized reward term).} The quantity the arm actually maximizes
--- ESMFold binder-monomer self-consistency sc-TM, measured on the arm's \emph{own} rollout groups and
logged each step --- climbs monotonically from the warm-start (Table~\ref{tab:esmfoldsctmprog}): sc-TM
more than doubles ($0.18\!\to\!0.47$) and the superposed C$\alpha$ RMSD more than halves
($14.0\!\to\!6.8$\,\AA), with the fold succeeding on every design (\ttk{scored\_frac}$=1.00$) and the
binder AA-composition entropy holding flat ($\approx\!3.9$ bits, no rollout collapse). \emph{The gradient
worked; the reward simply did not buy pass\%} --- so the step-$150$ regression in
Table~\ref{tab:esmfoldsctm} is a reward-quality failure, not a plumbing bug (the two look identical if
one only watches pass\%; \S\ref{sec:reward-weighting}). This is the direct contrast to the
\ttk{pae\_interface} arm, whose confidence source \emph{cleared} the offline gate at AUROC
$0.806$/$0.836$ where sc-TM did not. (These are training-rollout-sampler values; for reference the
base-A8 \emph{eval}-sampler sc-TM is $0.398$ --- a different sampler, so not directly comparable to the
rollout column.)

\ttk{esmfold/*} metrics; step $150$ is the Table~\ref{tab:esmfoldsctm} eval point). The optimized target
climbs steadily and the fold never fails --- confirming the z-scored gradient flows --- while pass\%

\paragraph{Current live arm: the BindCraft AF2 \ttk{pae\_interface} fold-confidence reward, z-scored.} The
newest live configuration
(\ttk{experiment/rl\_leflur\_binder\_grpo\_elj\_sparse\_clash\_chainbreak\_lc\_jaccard\_rog\_solelec\_bindcraftpae\_zscore},
warm-started from the combined sol$+$elec arm's step-$525$ checkpoint) replaces the Protenix
confidence source with a \textbf{BindCraft AF2} fold: $24$ single-GPU AF2 workers drain the fold
queue and return the interface PAE, and (now) \ttk{w\_pae\_interface\_scalar}$=1$ flips the \ttk{need\_conf} gate on
so every rollout group is folded. This supersedes the earlier flat variant
(\ttk{\ldots\_solelec\_bindcraftpae}, \ttk{w\_pae\_interface}$=1$), which folded the interface PAE into the
shared base-reward normalization; that arm was cancelled and replaced once we chose to give
\ttk{pae\_interface} its own unit-variance advantage channel. Its active reward weights split across the two combination paths of
\S\ref{sec:reward-weighting}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Base per-design reward}, summed flat and then group-standardized $(r-\mu)/(\sigma+\epsilon)$
as the ordinary GRPO advantage: the structure/geometry terms \ttk{w\_chainbreak}$=1$,
\ttk{w\_clash\_contact}$=1$ and \ttk{w\_rog}$=1$, the anti-degeneracy \ttk{w\_seq\_complexity}$=1$ and
\ttk{w\_struct\_complexity}$=1$, and the reward-side diversity
\ttk{w\_seq\_diversity}$=$\ttk{w\_struct\_diversity}$=0.1$ (3Di $k$-mer Jaccard). The flat fold-confidence
term is now off (\ttk{w\_pae\_interface}$=0$); the interface PAE has moved to its own z-score add-on below.
\item \emph{Isolated per-design z-score add-ons}, each standardized on its own within the group and
summed onto the advantage after the base standardization: $e_{lj}$ via \ttk{w\_elj\_scalar}$=1$, the
combined desolvation$+$electrostatics energy via \ttk{w\_solelec\_scalar}$=1$ (physical sub-weights
\ttk{w\_solelec\_wsol}$=$\ttk{w\_solelec\_welec}$=1.0$ inside the single z-score, Eq.~\ref{eq:solelec}),
and --- new in this arm --- the BindCraft AF2 interface PAE via \ttk{w\_pae\_interface\_scalar}$=1$. The
oriented confidence $\mathrm{clip}_{01}(1-\mathrm{PAE}/32)$ is positive-oriented (higher${=}$better), so
unlike the energy penalties it is \emph{not} negated before z-scoring.
\end{itemize}
\ttk{w\_ptm} is pinned to $0$ here (overriding the dataclass default $0.5$) so \ttk{pae\_interface} is
the \emph{sole} confidence driver; every other confidence, distribution, shape, ESMFold sc-TM, and
per-token weight is $0$. In contrast to every earlier arm, \ttk{pae\_interface} now \emph{is} given the
isolated per-design z-score treatment that $e_{lj}$ and sol$+$elec receive: rather than sharing one
group mean/std with the structure and diversity terms, it carries its own unit-variance advantage
channel. This is the direct response to the flat variant's dilution --- because \ttk{pae\_interface}
cleared the offline gate at the field-leading AUROC $0.806$/$0.836$ ($1$/$3$ recycles, above \ttk{e\_lj}
$0.720$), it earns an independent $\sigma$-unit rather than a diluted share of the shared normalization.

\textbf{Degeneracy watch.} A rising interface AAR together with an AA distribution racing away from the
Complexa reference (\ttk{dist/tv\_aa}, observed to climb $0.42\!\to\!0.59$ over the CHORD run) and a
falling per-design complexity ($\overline{\mathrm{LC}}$, \S\ref{sec:seqcomplex}) is the degenerate
outcome and kills an arm. \emph{Between-design novelty is not a reliable watch}:
\ttk{diversity/seq\_novelty\_mean} held $\approx\!0.91$ throughout the same run --- it is blind to
per-design collapse (\S\ref{sec:seqcomplex}). Health keys: \ttk{sft/ce\_loss} (should fall),
\ttk{dist/tv\_aa} (should not race away), $\overline{\mathrm{LC}}$/$\bar h$ (should not fall),
\ttk{ppo/grad\_norm} (SFT must not swamp the policy gradient), and for the per-token arm
\ttk{ppo/struct\_ratio\_init}$\approx1$, \ttk{ptclash/adv\_abs\_mean}.

\subsection{Generated-vs-predicted self-consistency: binder alone vs.\ complex}
\label{sec:sctmselfconsist}
The two evaluated z-score arms disagree with their own reward stories in exactly the place the
offline gate warned about --- the \emph{docking}. To read this directly we measure, for every one of
the $380$ designs, the TM-score between the \textbf{generated} backbone and the Table-6 eval's
\textbf{Protenix-predicted} refold of the same sequence, in two framings: (i) the binder monomer alone
(gen binder chain vs.\ predicted binder chain) and (ii) the whole antigen$+$binder complex (all
C$\alpha$, single rigid superposition, so the shared antigen anchors the frame and the number reflects
both the binder fold \emph{and} its docked placement). Gen designs are paired to predicted folds by
exact binder-sequence match within each target (all $380$ paired, none dropped), scored with
\ttk{tmtools} TM-align and target-balanced macro-averaged (Table~\ref{tab:sctmselfconsist}).

\centering

\begin{tabular}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{6.4cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{3.0cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{3.0cm}
                    >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{2.0cm}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Arm (Complexa-38, gen vs.\ Protenix refold)} & \textbf{TM binder-alone} $\uparrow$ & \textbf{TM complex} $\uparrow$ & $\boldsymbol{\Delta}$ \\
\midrule
ESMFold sc-TM z-score, step $1975$      & $\mathbf{0.778}\pm0.010$ & $\mathbf{0.580}\pm0.011$ & $0.198$\\
BindCraft AF2 \ttk{pae\_interface} z-score, step $1325$ & $0.569\pm0.012$ & $0.545\pm0.012$ & $\mathbf{0.024}$\\

\caption{\textbf{Self-consistency TM-score of the generated structure against its Protenix refold},
binder monomer alone vs.\ whole complex, on each arm's $380$-design Table-6 eval pool
($\pm$SEM over $38$ targets). $\Delta=$ TM$_{\text{binder}}-$TM$_{\text{complex}}$ is the fold-vs-dock
gap. The two arms fail in opposite ways. The \emph{ESMFold sc-TM} arm --- whose reward folds the binder
\emph{alone} --- drives the binder-monomer TM high ($0.778$) but the complex TM lags far behind
($0.580$, $\Delta=0.198$): the sequence reproduces the generated monomer, yet Protenix docks it
elsewhere, the docking-blind signature that keeps ipSAE at floor. The \emph{\ttk{pae\_interface}} arm,
rewarded on a docking-aware AF2 interface PAE, instead has a small gap ($\Delta=0.024$) --- monomer and
complex agree with each other --- but both TMs are lower ($0.569$/$0.545$): the surrogate keeps the fold
internally consistent without lifting the absolute agreement enough to clear the pass gate. Neither the
high-monomer/low-complex nor the consistent-but-low profile buys pass\%, which motivates the next arm:
take the binder-monomer TM from \textbf{ESMFold} (where it is already strong) and the \emph{complex} TM
from a docking-aware \textbf{BindCraft AF2} refold (the framing the ESMFold arm is blind to), rewarding
both self-consistencies at once.}
\label{tab:sctmselfconsist}
\end{table}

\subsection{The two-oracle TM reward: the first arm past the base bar}
\label{sec:twooracle}
The arm motivated at the close of \S\ref{sec:sctmselfconsist} --- take the binder-monomer TM from
\textbf{ESMFold} (where the docking-blind fold signal is already strong) and the \emph{complex} TM from
a docking-aware \textbf{BindCraft AF2} refold --- is now live and evaluated
(\ttk{experiment/rl\_leflur\_binder\_grpo\_elj\_sparse\_clash\_chainbreak\_lc\_jaccard\_rog\_solelec\_esmfoldbinder\_bindcraftcomplex\_tm\_zscore},
warm-started from the same sol$+$elec step-$525$ checkpoint as both parents). It adds \emph{two}
positive-oriented self-consistencies as isolated per-design z-score channels (\S\ref{sec:reward-weighting}):
the ESMFold binder sc-TM via \ttk{w\_sctm\_esmfold\_scalar}$=1$ (served by the shape/repack pool) and
--- new --- the whole-complex sc-TM against the BindCraft AF2 refold via
\ttk{w\_sctm\_complex\_scalar}$=1$ (riding the fold worker's returned \ttk{antigen\_xyz}/\ttk{binder\_xyz},
the antigen anchoring the joint superposition so the number reflects both fold \emph{and} docked
placement). The BindCraft \ttk{pae\_interface} term is \emph{off} in this arm
(\ttk{w\_pae\_interface}$=$\ttk{w\_pae\_interface\_scalar}$=0$): the complex signal here is a
\emph{TM-score}, not an interface PAE. The nine inherited base/energy terms ($e_{lj}$, sol$+$elec,
chain-break, clash-contact, $R_g$, LC$\times2$, Jaccard$\times2$) are byte-identical to both parents.

\textbf{Result: at its step-$575$ peak this is the first arm in the entire study to clear the base-A8
pass bar}, pass\% $\mathbf{6.32}$ vs.\ $5.61$ (Table~\ref{tab:twooracletable6}), and it does so
\emph{earlier} than either parent's best (ESMFold-only $5.79$ at step $1975$; the \ttk{pae} arm never
cleared the bar). The two-oracle self-consistency profile explains why (Table~\ref{tab:twooracletm}):
the ESMFold half lifts the binder-monomer TM to $0.681$ --- well above the \ttk{pae} arm's $0.569$ ---
while the docking-aware complex half keeps the complex TM at $0.573$ (essentially matching the
ESMFold-only arm's $0.580$) with a fold-vs-dock gap $\Delta=0.108$ roughly half the ESMFold-only arm's
$0.198$. It is the only one of the three fold arms whose \emph{high binder fold} and \emph{consistent
docked complex} co-occur, and the only one that converts them into pass\%.

\textbf{The margin oscillates but holds: base is cleared at two separated checkpoints.} A step-$600$
eval (just $25$ optimizer steps after the peak) dips \emph{below} base to pass\% $4.47$, with AAR
$0.256\!\to\!0.245$, $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{3Di}}$ $0.118\!\to\!0.103$ and ipSAE $0.080\!\to\!0.073$ all
softening --- the transient look of a learn-then-diverge slide. \textbf{But it recovers}: by step
$1125$ pass\% returns to $6.05$ (above base, just under the peak), and the recovery is \emph{not}
continued energy-hacking --- interface $e_{lj}$ \emph{rebounds} $3.35\!\to\!9.30$ (it does not keep
falling), iface $f$ moves \emph{into} the native band ($0.170\!\to\!0.146$), and AAR reaches its
trajectory maximum ($0.262$); s$1125$ is a healthier design state than the s$600$ dip, not a more
over-driven one (Table~\ref{tab:twooracletable6}). The self-consistency TMs corroborate the recovery:
they hold flat across the peak-to-dip ($0.681/0.573\to0.677/0.577$) and then \emph{rise} to their
trajectory maxima at s$1125$ (binder $0.768$, complex $0.594$ --- the complex TM even edges the
docking-blind ESMFold-only arm's $0.580$), so fold/dock agreement is strongest exactly where pass\%
recovers. This is
qualitatively unlike the sparse $e_{lj}+R_g$ arm, which peaked at step $300$ then \emph{monotonically}
eroded by $750$ (Table~\ref{tab:eljrogtraj}); here the base bar is cleared at both s$575$ and s$1125$
with only a one-checkpoint dip between. \textbf{Prefer the $\sim\!575$ peak for the single best
checkpoint}, but the durable read is that the two-oracle reward keeps the policy at-or-above base
rather than driving it away --- the $+0.44$--$0.71$\,pp margin is narrow ($\approx2$--$3$ designs) but
recurring. The persistent liability remains $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{3Di}}$ degeneracy ($\sim\!0.10$ at
every step vs.\ base $0.267$).

\emph{Protenix} per-token PAE (\ttk{scripts/\_grpo\_ipsae\_metrics.py}, target-macro), i.e.\ the same fold that
defines pass\%/pTM/ipTM --- \ttk{pae\_interaction} applies the ColabDesign/BindCraft \emph{definition} of mean
cross-chain interface PAE ($\tfrac12[\overline{\mathrm{PAE}}_{A\to B}+\overline{\mathrm{PAE}}_{B\to A}]$ over all
antigen--binder token pairs, no cutoff, lower $=$ more confident) to Protenix's PAE matrix; it is \emph{not} an
AF2/BindCraft fold; $e_{lj}$/\ttk{fa\_elec}/\ttk{fa\_sol} are the packed-interface
energies recomputed from each arm's side-chain shards. The arm was the first in the study over the base bar
at \textbf{step $575$} (pass\% $6.32$), with a broad backbone/diversity sweep vs.\ base --- $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$
$24.7\!\to\!6.8$, 3Di TV $0.437\!\to\!0.295$, break\% $28.1\!\to\!18.9$, SC $0.906\!\to\!0.925$,
$\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$ $0.519\!\to\!0.574$, iface $f$ toward native. The step-$600$ drop below base
(pass\% $4.47$) is a transient dip: by step $1125$ pass\% recovers to $6.05$ (above base) and \textbf{step
$1350$ reaches the trajectory high, pass\% $\mathbf{7.89}$} ($+2.3$ pp over base), clearing the base bar at
three separated checkpoints. The s$1350$ high is \emph{not} energy-hacking: both self-consistencies are strong
(TM binder $0.787$, TM complex $0.607$), $E_{\mathrm{clash}}$ stays far below base ($8.9$ vs.\ $24.7$), iface
$f$ ($0.162$), SC ($0.920$) and $R_g$ ($14.7$) hold in the improved band, and $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{AA}}$ is
high ($0.600$) --- pass\% is bought through fold/dock self-consistency, not packed-interface energetics
($e_{lj}$ $12.84$, \ttk{fa\_sol} $47.75$ are the \emph{trade}). \textbf{Steps $1625$ and $1950$ are a
reward-hacking divergence, not further progress:} pass\% \emph{erodes} to $4.47$ at both (back below the base
bar) even as the \emph{rewarded} binder self-consistency keeps climbing to its trajectory maximum (TM binder
$0.801\!\to\!\mathbf{0.805}$) and the complex TM plateaus at its maximum (TM complex $\mathbf{0.618}\!\to\!0.617$)
--- a textbook Goodhart split in which the policy keeps climbing its own TM oracles while the held-out Protenix
pass\% stalls below base. The mean confidences are \emph{not} a clean monotone fall (pTM
$0.702\!\to\!0.699\!\to\!\mathbf{0.705}$, ipTM $0.430\!\to\!0.415\!\to\!0.426$ tick back up by s$1950$), but the
\emph{fraction} clearing both thresholds does not recover: the confidence distribution shifts without converting
into more joint passes. \ttk{pae\_interaction} shows the disagreement is \emph{internal to the Protenix readout}
itself: the mean cross-chain interface PAE keeps \emph{sharpening} (falling) as training proceeds --- best at
s$1950$ ($\mathbf{16.47}$\,\AA), just past s$1350$/s$1625$ ($16.62$/$16.72$\,\AA) --- even though the joint
pTM/ipTM pass\% from the very same fold erodes. So the policy improves the average interface confidence Protenix
assigns while fewer designs actually clear the pass gate; the eroding pass\% is not an artifact of one skeptical
oracle. \textbf{So the arm's best read is s$1350$, not
its latest}; s$1625$--s$1950$ mark the stretch where the two-oracle TM reward saturates and trades dockability
for oracle self-agreement. \textbf{Persistent watch-items} across all steps: $\mathrm{LC}_{\mathrm{3Di}}$ collapse
($0.118/0.103/0.101/0.096/0.096/0.097$; the recurring 3Di-degeneracy floor), still-low ipSAE ($\le\!0.096$), and
$\Delta\Delta G$/$R_g$ trailing base. \textbf{Bold} $=$ best in row by direction (checkpoints only; base is
reference). The base A8 pTM/ipTM/$e_{lj}$/ipSAE references are pending a packed/PAE-dumped base pool.}
\label{tab:twooracletable6}
\end{table}

\section{Deliverable: a self-contained protein-RL post-training package}
\label{sec:package}
Everything above is \emph{one} instantiation --- a LeFlur binder policy against a specific reward
stack --- of a general capability: \textbf{post-training a token-based protein design model with GRPO
against composable, physically-grounded rewards, using differentiable trajectory log-probs and
out-of-process reward oracles}. Packaging that capability so a practitioner can \ttk{pip install} it
and post-train \emph{their own} protein language model or latent-structure-token model --- without
importing LeFlur, LBSTER, or our SLURM harness --- is a first-class deliverable. This section maps
what is already reusable, the couplings that must be cut, and the small set of interfaces that turn
the current code into an importable framework (working name \ttk{plm-design-rl}).

\subsection{What is already reusable}
The machinery is already largely factored into pure, dependency-light layers; the packaging work is
mostly \emph{declaring interfaces around them}, not rewriting them.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Reward terms} --- \ttk{lobster.rl\_training.rewards}. Every term (clash, chain-break,
  interface-distribution, diversity/linguistic-complexity, 3DZD shape complementarity, AAR, all-atom
  potentials, self-consistency TM, Protenix client) is a \emph{pure} numpy/torch module with no
  \ttk{trl} import and no dependency on the trainer loop --- the subpackage docstring already
  enforces this discipline (``\emph{Each module is pure \dots\ so the policy side can import the
  reward terms without pulling in the reward oracle's heavy deps}''). They are plain functions
  (\ttk{chainbreak\_reward}, \ttk{distribution\_terms}, \ttk{shape\_complementarity\_reward\_atoms},
  \ttk{lc\_saturating\_reward}), so the reward layer is \emph{already} a library.
\item \textbf{The differentiable log-prob kernel} --- \ttk{lobster.rl\_training.\_dfm\_logprob}
  (\ttk{dfm\_step\_prob} / \ttk{dfm\_step\_logprob} / \ttk{dfm\_step\_kl}). This rebuilds the
  absorbing-state discrete-flow-matching transition distribution out-of-place so gradients reach the
  head logits (``From reward to gradient'', \S\ref{sec:package} above). It is pure
  torch\,$+$\,bionemo --- the reusable ``differentiate the log-prob of a sampled generation'' engine.
\item \textbf{The trainer as configuration} --- \ttk{GRPOTrainerConfig} is already a declarative
  surface (per-term weights, per-metric clips, the CHORD $\mu$-blend, the per-token toggles, and the
  optimization knobs $G$/\ttk{rollout\_nsteps}/\ttk{mu}/\ttk{lr}/\ttk{eps\_clip}/\ttk{accum\_targets});
  \ttk{LeFlurGRPOTrainer} composes the reward client, the diversity terms, and the log-prob/KL
  kernels; \ttk{TargetSpec} declares one design target.
\item \textbf{The oracle transport} --- \ttk{ShapeRewardClient} (\ttk{submit\_group} /
  \ttk{collect\_group} / \ttk{rewards\_for\_group}) $+$ \ttk{scripts/ligandmpnn\_repack\_server.py}
  is a working async submit/block queue over a shared filesystem, with atomic-rename claiming so a
  worker pool of any size cooperates without coordination. This is the reusable pattern for running
  a heavy scorer (LigandMPNN, Protenix, ProteinMPNN) on its own accelerators off the policy's GPU.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The couplings to cut}
Four things currently prevent ``import and post-train my model'':
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The policy contract is implicit.} The trainer calls LeFlur methods \emph{by name}
  (\ttk{rollout\_with\_logprobs}, \ttk{\_recompute\_biased\_step\_logits},
  \ttk{logprob\_over\_trajectory}, \ttk{sequence\_sft\_loss},
  \ttk{logprob\_and\_sft\_over\_trajectory}) and hard-codes LeFlur's three tracks and their
  per-track vocab / mask-index conventions. No other model can be dropped in because there is no
  declared interface to implement.
\item \textbf{The log-prob is sampler-specific.} \ttk{\_dfm\_logprob} is correct for
  absorbing-state masked diffusion but not for autoregressive decoders or continuous diffusion. It
  must sit behind a ``log-prob provider'' the policy owns, so the sampler family is a plug, not a
  hard assumption.
\item \textbf{Reward composition lives inside the trainer.} The weighted, per-metric-clipped sum;
  the scalar-vs-per-token split; the CHORD $\mu$-blend; and the per-token advantage injection into
  the structure track are all trainer internals rather than a first-class, testable object a user
  can build and inspect.
\item \textbf{Oracles are wired ad hoc, and it all lives in the monorepo.} The queue directories,
  \ttk{.npz} payload schema, and the SLURM launch wrapper are bespoke; the pure core is only
  \emph{conventionally} separated from openfold / bionemo / Protenix. A distribution needs a
  declared \ttk{RewardPool} protocol and optional-extras dependency groups so the core installs with
  just torch.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Core abstractions}
Five small contracts turn the existing code into a framework. The policy owns \emph{how} it samples
and scores its own log-probs; the trainer owns the GRPO update; rewards are pure and composable;
heavy scorers hide behind a pool.

\begin{verbatim}
# plm_design_rl/api.py  -- the contracts a user implements or composes
from typing import Protocol, Sequence
import torch

class Trajectory(Protocol):
    """One stored rollout: sampled tokens + head logits per generation step, per track."""
    tracks: tuple[str, ...]                    # e.g. ("sequence","structure","tri") or ("seq",)
    steps: Sequence[dict]                      # step -> {track: (x_t, x_next, logits, t, dt)}
    gen_mask: dict[str, torch.Tensor]          # generated (vs inpainted/fixed) positions

class Policy(Protocol):
    """A post-trainable design model. LeFlur becomes ONE adapter; BYO-model implements this."""
    tracks: tuple[str, ...]
    vocab_size: dict[str, int]
    mask_index: dict[str, int]                 # sampler metadata the update needs

    def rollout(self, cond, *, nsteps: int, **sampler_kw) -> Trajectory: ...
    def logprob(self, traj: Trajectory, steps: Sequence[int], *, biased: bool) -> torch.Tensor: ...
    def sft_logits(self, traj: Trajectory, steps: Sequence[int]) -> torch.Tensor | None: ...

class Reward(Protocol):
    name: str
    per_token: bool                            # scalar advantage vs dense per-residue credit
    def __call__(self, designs: list["Design"]) -> "RewardOut": ...

class RewardPool(Protocol):                    # out-of-process oracle (heavy scorer)
    def submit_group(self, target_id: str, designs: list["Design"]) -> dict: ...
    def collect_group(self, handle: dict) -> list[dict | None]: ...

# RewardStack composes weighted, per-metric-clipped terms (scalar + per-token) into one
# reward; GRPOTrainer(policy, reward_stack, config) owns the Dr.GRPO update, advantage
# normalization, PPO clip, the CHORD mu-blend, and per-token advantage injection.
\end{verbatim}

The DFM kernel is the reference \ttk{Policy.logprob} implementation (a
\ttk{MaskedDiffusionPolicy} mixin wrapping \ttk{dfm\_step\_logprob}); an
\ttk{AutoregressivePolicy} mixin (teacher-forced decoder log-prob) is the second reference so the
package is not diffusion-only. \ttk{sft\_logits} is optional --- returning \ttk{None} disables the
CHORD term, so distillation is a capability, not a requirement.

\subsection{Package layout and optional extras}
A standalone distribution with a torch-only core and heavy scorers behind extras:
\begin{verbatim}
plm_design_rl/
  api.py                 # the Protocols above (Policy/Trajectory/Reward/RewardPool)
  logprob/               # dfm.py (from _dfm_logprob), autoregressive.py   [core: torch]
  rewards/               # clash, chainbreak, distribution, diversity, complexity,
                         #   potentials, shape_sc, aar, structure_sc  [core: numpy/scipy]
                         #   ddg (tmol interface dG)                   [extra: tmol]
  stack.py               # RewardStack: weights, per-metric clips, scalar/per-token merge
  pool/                  # queue.py (from ShapeRewardClient) + server entry points
  trainers/grpo.py       # GRPOTrainer + GRPOConfig  (from _leflur_grpo_trainer)
  sft.py                 # CHORD phi-weighted CE mu-blend  (from _structure_sft)
  adapters/leflur.py     # LeFlur -> Policy adapter (reference)
extras:
  [ligandmpnn]  openfold + LigandMPNN   -> repack/potentials/shape/AAR oracles
  [protenix]    protenix               -> co-folding confidence + self-consistency oracle
  [tmol]        tmol                   -> interface dG (beta_nov2016) potential upgrade
  [trl]         trl                    -> the UME text-GRPO path (unchanged)
\end{verbatim}
The core (\ttk{api} $+$ \ttk{logprob} $+$ \ttk{rewards} that need only numpy/scipy $+$ \ttk{stack}
$+$ \ttk{trainers}) installs with torch alone; a reward whose oracle needs openfold/Protenix/tmol
raises a clear ``\ttk{pip install plm-design-rl[ligandmpnn]}'' error at construction, never at import.
(\ttk{tmol} is distributed as wheels built per \ttk{python}$\times$\ttk{torch}$\times$\ttk{CUDA}, so
the \ttk{[tmol]} lane must match the local torch build --- e.g.\ \ttk{+cu128torch2.8 cp312} on GPU or
\ttk{+cpu} for a CPU-only scoring pool.)

\subsection{Bring your own model}
The user implements \ttk{Policy} for their model, picks reward terms, and trains --- no LeFlur code
on the path:
\begin{verbatim}
from plm_design_rl import GRPOTrainer, GRPOConfig, RewardStack
from plm_design_rl.rewards import (InterfacePotential, InterfaceDDG, ChainBreak,
                                   ChordSFT, LinguisticComplexity)
from plm_design_rl.logprob import MaskedDiffusionPolicy
from my_models import MyStructureTokenPLM          # your checkpoint

class MyPolicy(MaskedDiffusionPolicy):             # inherits logprob() from the DFM kernel
    tracks = ("sequence", "structure_tokens")
    ...                                            # implement rollout() with your sampler

policy  = MyPolicy(MyStructureTokenPLM.from_pretrained("ckpt"))
rewards = RewardStack([
    (1.0, InterfacePotential(term="e_lj", per_token=True)),   # dense structure-track credit
    (0.0, InterfaceDDG(flavor="ddg", per_token=True)),        # tmol dG upgrade (opt-in [tmol])
    (1.0, ChainBreak(gate="count")),                          # backbone realism
    (0.1, ChordSFT(expert="ligandmpnn", scope="binder")),     # optional seq distillation
    (1.0, LinguisticComplexity(lc_full=0.7)),                 # anti-degeneracy
])
trainer = GRPOTrainer(policy, rewards,
    GRPOConfig(group_size=64, rollout_nsteps=40, mu=2, lr=1e-5, accum_targets=10))
trainer.fit(targets)
\end{verbatim}
Swapping \ttk{tracks} to \ttk{("sequence",)} makes the same call post-train a sequence-only PLM;
adding an oracle-backed term (\ttk{ProtenixConfidence(...)}) transparently spins up the reward pool.
The knobs are exactly today's live config --- the deliverable is the \emph{interface}, not new
learning machinery.

\caption{\textbf{The \ttk{plm\_design\_rl} package: one orchestrator, everything else swapped behind a
protocol --- and both compute-heavy ends scale out.} \emph{Components.} \ttk{GRPOTrainer} (center) is
the sole orchestrator --- it holds the \ttk{GRPOConfig} and runs the optimization (Dr.GRPO objective,
PPO-clip, $\mu$-blended SFT auxiliary loss, per-token advantages); everything else plugs into it. It
is drawn as a \emph{stack} because the trainer runs single-node data-parallel over $W$ GPU ranks
(\S\ref{sec:multigpu}): each rank processes a round-robin shard of the step's \ttk{accum\_targets}
targets, accumulates its \emph{unscaled} local gradient, and the ranks all-reduce (SUM) the policy
gradient and a live-target count and divide by the global count (the self-loop) --- so every replica
steps the identical, exactly-averaged gradient. \ttk{Policy} (upper left) is the swappable model seam:
any PLM or structure-token model implementing \ttk{rollout()} (sample a design trajectory) and
\ttk{logprob()} (differentiable per-step log-probability). The \ttk{logprob} kernel (lower left) is the
reusable DFM/autoregressive implementation the \ttk{Policy} \emph{inherits} --- a component of it, not
a pipeline stage. \ttk{RewardStack} (upper right) aggregates the reward terms into a weighted, clipped
signal with both scalar and per-token parts; \ttk{RewardPool} (lower right, also a stack) is the oracle
backend --- $M$ interchangeable runners (LigandMPNN repack, Protenix fold) on their own accelerators,
off the training GPUs. The top node carries the run inputs (\ttk{fit(targets)}, config, extras).
\emph{Flow.} The trainer's outgoing calls ride the top arc of each pair and returns ride the bottom
arc: config drives the trainer; the trainer samples through the \ttk{Policy} and gets back the
trajectory and its differentiable $\log\pi_\theta$; those designs go to the \ttk{RewardStack}, which
offloads heavy terms to the \ttk{RewardPool} through a \emph{shared filesystem queue} --- any of the
$M$ runners claims a job by atomic rename, so the pool cooperates without coordination and all $W$
ranks submit to the one queue with no collision. The trainer turns the returned scalar\,$+$\,per-token
rewards into advantages and pushes the policy gradient $A_i\,\nabla\log\pi_\theta$ back onto the
\ttk{Policy}'s weights --- once per step, after the cross-rank all-reduce. The dashed edge is the only
non-data-flow arrow: the kernel \emph{supplies} \ttk{logprob()} to the \ttk{Policy}.}
\label{fig:package}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Scaling the trainer: single-node data parallelism}
\label{sec:multigpu}
The GRPO update is embarrassingly parallel across the \ttk{accum\_targets} targets it averages per
optimizer step: each target's rollout, scoring, and advantage are independent --- advantages
standardize \emph{within} a target's group of $G$, never across targets (\S\ref{sec:reward2grad}) ---
so the only cross-target coupling is the final gradient mean. The trainer therefore carries a
single-node data-parallel mode that turns the serial per-step target loop into a wall-clock speedup on
the multi-GPU boxes the runs already sit on (the $8\times$b200 nodes leave $7$ GPUs idle today),
\emph{without} changing the effective batch: \ttk{accum\_targets} is reinterpreted as a \emph{global}
count and sharded round-robin across ranks (\ttk{specs\_all[rank::world\_size]}), so $W$ ranks each
process $\approx\!\ttk{accum\_targets}/W$ targets and the step runs $\approx\!W\times$ faster. It is a
speedup, not a bigger batch --- the optimizer sees the same mean gradient over the same
\ttk{accum\_targets} targets.

Correctness is exact, not statistical. Each rank runs its shard's backward passes \emph{unscaled}
(accumulating $\sum_{\text{local}}\nabla$); the trainer then all-reduces (SUM) the policy gradients
together with a scalar live-target count and divides by the \emph{global} count, so the reduced
gradient --- identical on every rank --- equals the single-GPU mean over all \ttk{accum\_targets} live
targets regardless of how unevenly the shard splits. SUM-then-divide (not a mean all-reduce) is what
makes an uneven split ($3$ live on one rank, $2$ on another) weight correctly; an all-flat shard
contributes zero gradient and zero count, and a globally all-flat step is skipped identically on every
rank (no deadlock). The reduction is over the policy's trainable parameters, which keeps it
model-agnostic and is why it is a manual all-reduce rather than a DDP module wrap: the trainer drives
the model through \ttk{rollout}/\ttk{logprob}, never the module's \ttk{\_\_call\_\_}, so DDP's autograd
hooks would never fire --- the explicit reduce is both simpler and the only correct option here.

The mode is additive and opt-in --- gated on \ttk{world\_size}$>1$, so the single-GPU path stays
byte-identical and every live run is untouched, on-run and on-restart. Nothing else in the package
changes: the filesystem reward queue already claims jobs by atomic rename with \ttk{uuid} ids, so $W$
ranks share one \ttk{RewardPool} with no per-rank prefix; the checkpoint stays a plain unwrapped
\ttk{state\_dict} (no wrapper to strip on load); logging and checkpointing are rank-$0$-gated behind a
barrier; and the per-rank RNG is offset so the shards decorrelate while the target \emph{schedule}
stays shared. In the package this is a single \ttk{GRPOConfig} field
(\ttk{world\_size}/\ttk{rank}/\ttk{local\_rank}), populated from the launcher's \ttk{torchrun}
environment --- a user post-training their own \ttk{Policy} gets the same $W\times$ speedup by
launching under \ttk{torchrun -\/-nproc\_per\_node=$W$} with no change to their model or reward code.

\subsection{Throughput and GPU idle: sizing the two-pool reward pipeline}
\label{sec:throughput}
Once the policy is data-parallel (\S\ref{sec:multigpu}), the wall-clock floor moves \emph{off} the
GPUs that hold the policy and \emph{onto} the reward pool: every design in a step is scored by an
external oracle over the shared-filesystem queue, and the optimizer step cannot close until the
slowest shard of that scoring wave returns. The combined-oracle arm makes the cost concrete --- each
of the $\ttk{accum\_targets}\times G = 10\times64 = 640$ designs per step needs \emph{both} a
BindCraft/AF2 complex fold (the docking-aware complex sc-TM, $\sim\!44$\,s at $3$ recycles) \emph{and}
a LigandMPNN repack$+$ESMFold pass (interface energies $+$ the binder sc-TM). The schematic traces one
step, marks where a GPU can go idle (\textcolor{bLine}{amber}), and names the knob that closes each
gap. \textbf{The fold pool is the bottleneck}: its GPU count sets the step floor.

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\footnotesize,>={Stealth[length=2mm]},
  pol/.style ={draw=ioLine,fill=ioFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.4cm},
  q/.style   ={draw=dLine,fill=dTint,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.4cm},
  fold/.style={draw=bLine,fill=bFill,line width=1.3pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=5.9cm},
  shape/.style={draw=aLine,fill=aFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=5.9cm},
  rew/.style ={draw=lfLine,fill=lfFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.4cm},
  bwd/.style ={draw=ioLine,fill=ioFill,line width=0.8pt,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=4pt,text width=12.4cm},
  lever/.style={draw=bLine,fill=bTint,line width=0.6pt,dashed,rounded corners,align=left,inner sep=3pt,font=\scriptsize,text width=5.4cm},
  fl/.style={->,thick,ioLine},fj/.style={->,thick,lfLine},fb/.style={->,thick,dashed,ioLine}]

\node[pol] (pol) at (0,0)
  {\textbf{policy node}\quad\ttk{torchrun -\/-nproc\_per\_node=NRANKS} (one b200 node, \ttk{NRANKS}$=8$)\\[1pt]
   rollout: $\ttk{accum\_targets}$ sharded round-robin across ranks $\Rightarrow$ all ranks generate
   \emph{in parallel} $\to 10\times64=640$ designs/step\hfill\textcolor{ioLine}{\itshape gen collapses $\sim\!N\times$ with ranks}};
\node[q] (q) at (0,-1.95)
  {\textbf{shared-filesystem queue}\quad atomic-rename \ttk{claim\_one()} by \ttk{uuid} (one pool, no per-rank prefix)\\[1pt]
   each design fans out to \emph{two} oracle wants\hfill\ttk{cache:true} $\Rightarrow$ identical designs scored once};

\node[fold] (fold) at (-3.4,-4.65)
  {\textbf{FOLD pool}\ \textcolor{bLine}{(bottleneck)}\\ BindCraft/AF2 $\to$ \ttk{antigen\_xyz}/\ttk{binder\_xyz}\\[2pt]
   \ttk{N\_WORKERS}$=40$ GPUs, \emph{static} b200\\ $\sim\!44$\,s/fold @ $3$ recycles\\[2pt]
   \textbf{step floor} $\approx \dfrac{640}{\ttk{N\_WORKERS}/44\text{s}}$\\[1pt]
   $40\Rightarrow\!\sim\!11$\,min $\approx$ gen (balanced)};
\node[shape] (shape) at (3.4,-4.65)
  {\textbf{SHAPE pool}\\ LigandMPNN repack $+$ ESMFold\\[2pt]
   \ttk{M\_SHAPE}$=24$ GPUs (a10g)\\ wants: \ttk{pot} ($e_{\mathrm{LJ}}$, sol$+$elec) $+$\\ \ttk{esmfold} (binder sc-TM)\\[2pt]
   cheaper/design $\Rightarrow$ not the floor};

\node[rew] (rew) at (0,-7.35)
  {\textbf{reward assembly} (rank-local)\quad per-metric clip $\to$ $z$-score each scalar within the group of $G$\\[1pt]
   complex sc-TM (fold) $+$ binder sc-TM (shape) $+$ 9 base terms $\to$ design-level advantage $A$};
\node[bwd] (bwd) at (0,-8.95)
  {\textbf{backward} unscaled per shard $\to$ \ttk{all\_reduce(SUM)} grads $+$ live-count $\to$ divide by global count $\to$ \ttk{step()} (\S\ref{sec:multigpu})};

\node[lever] (lv1) at (-3.4,-11.15)
  {\textbf{keep fold busy \& matched to gen}\\ $\bullet$ \ttk{REWARD\_NSHARDS}$=$\ttk{N\_WORKERS}$=40$ --- else extra workers never \ttk{claim}\\
   $\bullet$ size \ttk{N\_WORKERS} so fold wave $\approx$ gen\\ $\bullet$ \emph{static} b200 (a10g autoscaler churns $\to$ dead shards $\to$ idle)\\
   $\bullet$ warm-resident: \ttk{MAX\_IDLE\_S}$=3600 >$ step gap (no per-step model reload)\\ $\bullet$ \ttk{flock} AF2 params once/node};
\node[lever] (lv2) at (3.4,-11.15)
  {\textbf{absorb the rank burst / avoid deadlock}\\ $\bullet$ \ttk{M\_SHAPE}$\sim\!\ttk{NRANKS}\times$ shards --- all ranks submit near-simultaneously\\
   $\bullet$ \ttk{shape.timeout\_s}$=600$: a hung shard is dropped, not a wedged $8$-way NCCL wait\\
   $\bullet$ \ttk{reward.timeout\_s}$=1800$ for the slower fold\\ $\bullet$ \ttk{0.0}$\to$\ttk{NaN} on a failed fold neutralizes (never punishes) via the $z$-score};

\draw[fl] (pol) -- (q);
\draw[fl] (q.south) -- (fold.north);
\draw[fl] (q.south) -- (shape.north);
\draw[fj] (fold.south) -- (rew.north);
\draw[fj] (shape.south) -- (rew.north);
\draw[fl] (rew) -- (bwd);
\draw[fb] (bwd.west) to[out=180,in=180,looseness=1.5] node[left,font=\scriptsize,text width=1.9cm]{updated $\theta$ (next step)} (pol.west);
\draw[fb,bLine] (fold.south) -- (lv1.north);
\draw[fb,aLine] (shape.south) -- (lv2.north);

% pipeline-overlap callout spanning gen and fold
\node[lever,fill=dTint,draw=dLine,text width=5.0cm] (lv3) at (7.9,-3.1)
  {\textbf{hide the fold wait}\\ pipeline reward-wait: log-prob recompute $+$ backward-prep run \emph{during} the fold wave $\Rightarrow$ step $\approx \max(\text{gen},\text{fold})+\text{bwd}$, not the sum};
\draw[fb,dLine] (fold.east) -- (lv3.west);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}

\noindent The idle-time budget follows the boxes. \textbf{(1) On b200 the fold pool is no longer the
ceiling --- the shape pool is.} A step folds $640$ complexes; on b200 an AF2 fold is $\approx\!0.5$\,s/design,
so only $\sim\!6$ live b200 put the fold wave at $\approx\!53$\,s, matched to the shape pool (repack $+$
ESMFold, $\approx\!2$\,s/design on $24$ a10g $=\approx\!53$\,s), which then becomes the floor. Past that
match point more fold b200 just idle; the throughput lever moves to the shape pool. (On the old a10g
fold pool a fold was $\sim\!44$\,s/design and $40$ workers put the wave at $\sim\!11$\,min, so \emph{fold}
was the ceiling --- b200 is $\sim\!90\times$ cheaper per fold and inverts this.) The live failure mode is
the opposite of undersizing: the b200 fold array degraded from $40$ requested to $3$ alive ($37$ FAILED
on a \ttk{claim\_one()} stat race, see (5)), which re-inflates the wave to $\approx\!107$\,s and makes the
step fold-bound at $\approx\!147$\,s --- the fix is keeping $\sim\!6$ alive, not adding GPUs (Fig.~\ref{fig:gpuutil}).
\textbf{(2) \ttk{REWARD\_NSHARDS} must equal \ttk{N\_WORKERS}.} A group is split into
\ttk{reward.n\_shards} shards; leave it at the default $8$ with $40$ workers and $32$ workers never
\ttk{claim} a job --- pure idle silicon ([[grpo-multigpu-speedup-pool-scaling]]). \textbf{(3) The shape
pool is scaled to the rank burst, not to per-design cost:} all \ttk{NRANKS} ranks submit their groups
near-simultaneously, so \ttk{M\_SHAPE}$\sim\!\ttk{NRANKS}\times$ the shards keeps the whole burst in one
wave. \textbf{(4) Workers stay warm.} \ttk{MAX\_IDLE\_S}$=3600$ exceeds the inter-step gap so AF2 and
ESMFold stay resident --- a shorter idle-exit re-pays the multi-second model load every step, and on
the a10g autoscaler a churned worker also re-streams the $\sim\!5$\,GB AF2 params. \textbf{(5) Static
b200 over a10g for the fold pool --- with a race-safe claim.} A b200 AF2 fold ($\approx\!0.5$\,s) is
$\sim\!90\times$ cheaper than on a10g and the static b200 nodes don't churn like the a10g autoscaler.
The pool's real fragility was a \ttk{claim\_one()} stat race: with many workers polling one \ttk{new/}
dir, a peer renames a \ttk{*.json} between the \ttk{glob} and the \ttk{stat} in the sort key, so an
unguarded \ttk{p.stat().st\_mtime} raises \ttk{FileNotFoundError} and crashes the worker --- this
felled $35$ of $40$ at launch (the $3/40$ in Fig.~\ref{fig:gpuutil}b), which is what makes the step
fold-bound. Guard the sort-key \ttk{stat} (skip vanished files; the \ttk{os.rename} stays the real
claim, [[grpo-shape-pool-claimone-race]]) and \ttk{flock}-stage the AF2 params once per node; the
pool then holds at $\sim\!6$ live b200, matched to the shape floor. \textbf{(6) Timeouts protect the step, not just the
worker:} \ttk{shape.timeout\_s}$=600$ drops a hung shard rather than wedging the $8$-way NCCL
all-reduce that follows ([[grpo-8way-nccl-hang-pool-starvation]]), \ttk{reward.timeout\_s}$=1800$ gives
the slower fold room, and a failed fold maps \ttk{0.0}$\to$\ttk{NaN} so the $z$-score \emph{neutralizes}
it instead of injecting a spurious gradient. \textbf{(7) The fold wait is hidden:} the log-prob
recompute and backward-prep overlap the fold wave (pipeline reward-wait), so a balanced step costs
$\approx\max(\text{gen},\text{fold})+\text{backward}$ rather than their sum. Net: with fold$\approx$gen
and the pool sized to the shards and the burst, no stage waits on a stage that could have been running.

\subsection{GPU utilization: target schedule vs.\ observed}
\label{sec:gpuutil}
The pipeline schematic of \S\ref{sec:throughput} names \emph{where} a GPU can go idle; this one shows
\emph{when}, as an occupancy timeline of a single optimizer step across the three GPU groups (policy
ranks, fold pool, shape pool). Colored $=$ doing useful work; \textcolor{bLine}{amber, dashed} $=$
silicon idle. On b200 a fold is cheap ($\approx\!0.5$\,s/design), so a healthy fold pool clears the
$640$-fold wave in one $\sim\!8$\,s burst and the a10g \emph{shape} pool (repack$+$ESMFold,
$\approx\!2$\,s/design) becomes the floor. Panel~(a) is that shape-bound target: gen,
the pipelined log-prob recompute and the quick fold wave all finish early, the step costs
$\approx\text{gen}+\text{shape}+\text{bwd}\approx92$\,s, and any fold b200 beyond the $\sim\!6$ needed to
match the shape wave simply idle. Panel~(b) is the regime the run is actually in now, after both pools
were brought up to size: a \ttk{claim\_one()} stat race had felled $37$ of $40$ fold workers at launch
(since guarded), and $+3$ cooperative b200 workers on the same queue restore the fold pool to $\sim\!12$
live; \ttk{M\_SHAPE} was then raised $+24\to48$ a10g. Both pools now drain each wave with \emph{zero}
standing backlog and the step holds at $\approx\!113$\,s --- \textbf{near-floor}, $\sim\!1.23\times$ (a).
Crucially, scaling the shape pool $24\to48$ did \emph{not} drop the step further: the reward assembles
only when the \emph{slowest} shard returns, so the $\approx\!21$\,s residual over (a)'s $92$\,s ideal is
that slowest-shard tail plus gen$+$bwd$+$queue variance --- not shape rounds --- and more workers of
either kind are now marginal. The remaining lever toward (a) is a lighter/faster fold or a larger
\ttk{accum\_targets}, not more GPUs ([[grpo-throughput-tracking-tool]], [[grpo-multigpu-speedup-pool-scaling]]).

\caption{\textbf{GPU-utilization timeline of one GRPO optimizer step} of the two-oracle TM run
(config \ttk{...\_esmfoldbinder\_bindcraftcomplex\_tm\_zscore}, $640$ designs/step $=$ \ttk{accum\_targets}
$10\times$ \ttk{group\_size} $64$), three GPU groups on the vertical axis, wall-clock \emph{seconds} on
the horizontal. Solid colored bars are useful work; \textcolor{bLine}{amber dashed-outlined, hatched}
boxes marked \textsc{idle} are idle silicon. Measured anchors: gen $\approx\!18$\,s ($8$ b200 ranks in
parallel); a b200 AF2 fold $\approx\!0.5$\,s/design; a shape unit (LigandMPNN repack $+$ ESMFold)
$\approx\!2$\,s/design; backward
$+$ all\_reduce $+$ step $\approx\!21$\,s. \textbf{(a) Fold pool healthy --- shape-bound.} A healthy
b200 fold pool clears the $640$-fold wave in one $\sim\!8$\,s burst, so the shape wave becomes the floor
and the step costs $\approx\text{gen}+\text{shape}+\text{bwd}\approx92$\,s; every fold b200 beyond the
$\sim\!6$ needed to match the shape wave simply idles. \textbf{(b) Observed (current) --- near-floor.}
The fold b200 array had collapsed to $3$ of $40$ requested workers ($37$ FAILED on a \ttk{claim\_one()}
stat race --- a peer renaming a queued \ttk{new/*.json} between the \ttk{glob} and the \ttk{stat} in the
sort key raised an unguarded \ttk{FileNotFoundError} and crashed the worker;
[[grpo-shape-pool-claimone-race]]), so the $640$ folds crawled through $3$ GPUs in $\approx\!107$\,s and
stretched the step to $\approx\!277/154/140$\,s. Two cooperative adds on the same queues (no policy
change, no relaunch --- atomic-rename claiming lets a new array just join) brought both pools up to size:
$+7$ then $+3$ b200 fold workers restore fold to $\sim\!12$ live (the $640$-fold wave clears in
$\approx\!27$\,s at the \emph{measured} $0.5$\,s/design), and \ttk{M\_SHAPE} was raised $+24\to48$ a10g.
Both pools now drain each wave with \emph{zero} standing backlog, and throughput rose
$\sim\!16.9$k$\to\!\sim\!20.4$k designs/hr. But the step settles at $\approx\!113$\,s, essentially
unchanged by the shape scale-up: doubling the shape pool $24\to48$ cut the mean rounds-per-worker
(from $\sim\!3$ to $\sim\!1.7$) yet the reward assembles only when the \emph{slowest} shard returns, so
the $\approx\!21$\,s residual over (a)'s $92$\,s ideal is that slowest-shard tail plus gen$+$bwd$+$queue
variance --- a \textbf{near-floor} regime, \emph{not} a fold regression ((a)'s $0.5$\,s/design fold anchor
is confirmed, fold backlog $0$). The lever to approach (a) is therefore no longer worker count (both
backlogs are $0$; extra workers of either kind only idle) but a lighter/faster fold or a larger
\ttk{accum\_targets} that amortizes the fixed per-wave tail
([[grpo-throughput-tracking-tool]], [[grpo-multigpu-speedup-pool-scaling]]).}
\label{fig:gpuutil}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Scope to ship}
The path is incremental and testable at each step: (1) lift \ttk{rewards} $+$ \ttk{\_dfm\_logprob}
$+$ the queue client into \ttk{plm\_design\_rl} unchanged (they are already pure); (2) extract
\ttk{RewardStack} and the \ttk{GRPOConfig}/optimization half of \ttk{GRPOTrainerConfig} from the
trainer; (3) define the \ttk{Policy}/\ttk{RewardPool} protocols and refactor
\ttk{LeFlurGRPOTrainer} to call them, with an \ttk{adapters/leflur.py} that re-expresses today's
methods as the protocol (regression-tested against the live run for byte-identical rewards and
\ttk{ratio\_init}$\approx1$); (4) publish extras and a \ttk{README} with the two examples above.
Steps 1--2 are pure moves; step 3 is the only real interface work, and the LeFlur adapter is the
proof that the abstraction is faithful.

\subsection{Implementation status}
\textbf{Step 1 is complete} (commit \ttk{043f0f8}, \ttk{complex\_infra}); steps 2--4 remain.

\paragraph{Done (Step 1 --- pure moves).} The in-repo package \ttk{src/plm\_design\_rl/} now exists
and is auto-discovered by \ttk{setuptools} (no \ttk{pyproject} edit needed). The pure layers were
lifted out of \ttk{lobster.rl\_training} \emph{byte-identically}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \ttk{logprob/dfm.py} $\leftarrow$ \ttk{\_dfm\_logprob.py} (the DFM \ttk{dfm\_step\_prob} /
  \ttk{dfm\_step\_logprob} / \ttk{dfm\_step\_kl} kernels);
\item \ttk{rewards/\_*.py} $\leftarrow$ the ten reward-term modules (protenix, structure,
  diversity, distribution, clash, chainbreak, sc\_clash, aar, shape, protenix\_structure\_expert);
\item \ttk{pool/queue.py} $\leftarrow$ \ttk{\_shape\_reward\_pool.py} (\ttk{ShapeRewardClient} /
  \ttk{reward\_from\_shape}), with its one internal import repointed to
  \ttk{plm\_design\_rl.rewards.\_protenix\_reward}.
\end{itemize}
Package \ttk{\_\_init\_\_} files publish the reward terms, the DFM kernels, and the pool clients
(\ttk{ShapeRewardClient}, \ttk{ProtenixRewardClient}, \ttk{reward\_from\_shape},
\ttk{atomic\_write\_json}, \ttk{ensure\_queue}). Every historical
\ttk{lobster.rl\_training} import path is preserved by \textbf{transparent \ttk{sys.modules}-alias
shims} so old code resolves to the \emph{same object} (each moved submodule) and the reward package
namespace is re-exported unchanged. \ttk{LeFlurGRPOTrainer}, \ttk{\_structure\_sft}, and
\ttk{reward\_functions} were \emph{not touched} --- their \ttk{from .rewards} / \ttk{from
.\_dfm\_logprob} imports transparently get the moved implementations. Verified: \ttk{ruff} clean;
new \ttk{tests/plm\_design\_rl/test\_package\_and\_shims.py} (27 tests: import success $+$
old-path$\leftrightarrow$new-path object identity) plus the full \ttk{rl\_training} suite pass (306
total). The refactor lives in the \ttk{complex\_infra} worktree and could not perturb the live
run, whose processes import from a different worktree and editable install.

\paragraph{Also done (the tmol interface-\texorpdfstring{$\Delta\Delta G$}{ddG} reward, packaged).}
The physics-complete upgrade of the numpy \ttk{e\_lj} proxy (\S\ref{sec:potentials}) now ships
\emph{as a package reward module}, \ttk{plm\_design\_rl.rewards.\_ddg\_reward}, so it is wired
exactly like every other term the moment its offline gate clears. It computes the interface
$\Delta\Delta G$ of binding with tmol's full \ttk{beta\_nov2016} energy (adding the
\ttk{fa\_lk}/\ttk{lk\_ball} desolvation the numpy proxy lacks) in two flavors --- \emph{rigid}
(\ttk{ddg\_*}: cross-chain interaction energy of the relaxed bound complex) and
\emph{unbound-relaxed} (\ttk{ddg\_ub\_*}: each separated partner independently Cartesian-minimized,
adding strain-relief-on-unbinding) --- plus a bounded logistic reward mapper (more-negative
$\Delta\Delta G$ $\to$ higher reward $\in[0,1]$) and a contact-weighted per-binder-residue
attribution for dense per-token credit. It observes the extra discipline: \ttk{torch} is the only
top-level import, \ttk{tmol} is imported lazily, and the first scoring call without it raises a
clear ``\ttk{pip install plm-design-rl[tmol]}'' error --- never at import. The \ttk{[tmol]} extra is
declared in \ttk{pyproject.toml}; a \ttk{sys.modules}-alias shim preserves the
\ttk{lobster.rl\_training.rewards.\_ddg\_reward} path; the offline validation drivers
(\ttk{scripts/\_tmol\_ddg\_compute.py} / \ttk{\_analyze.py}) now re-export the engine from the
package (single source of truth); and \ttk{tests/plm\_design\_rl/test\_ddg\_reward.py} checks the
mapper (bounds, monotonicity, floor-to-zero, numerical stability) and the install-error discipline,
with the shim-identity suite extended to cover it. Whether the weight is turned on above $0$ is
gated on the offline head-to-head (\S\ref{sec:ddg}): the matched $811$-design run is now
\emph{done}, and it is a null --- on a single-checkpoint set every interface-energy term (numpy
$\R_{\mathrm{LJ}}$ included) collapses to $\approx\!0.5$, so no $\Delta\Delta G$ flavor decisively
beats \ttk{e\_lj}. The term therefore \textbf{ships at weight $0$} (tracked diagnostic, drop-in
ready), pending a wide-pool re-test on the base$\rightarrow$GRPO span where \ttk{e\_lj} reaches
$0.778$.

\paragraph{Also done (single-node multi-GPU data parallelism).}
The scaling mode of \S\ref{sec:multigpu} is implemented and tested in \ttk{complex\_infra}, additive
and gated on \ttk{world\_size}$>1$ so the live single-GPU runs are byte-identical and untouched.
\ttk{GRPOTrainerConfig} gains \ttk{world\_size}/\ttk{rank}/\ttk{local\_rank} (defaulting to the
single-GPU path); \ttk{\_leflur\_grpo\_trainer} shards \ttk{accum\_targets} round-robin, accumulates
\emph{unscaled} local gradients across its shard, and reduces them through a new
\ttk{\_allreduce\_and\_scale\_encoder\_grads} helper (SUM the grads $+$ SUM a live-count scalar,
divide by the global count) before the existing clip/step; \ttk{cmdline/rl\_train} initializes the
NCCL process group from the \ttk{torchrun} environment (\ttk{set\_device} \emph{before}
\ttk{init\_process\_group} and \ttk{model.to}), offsets the per-rank RNG while keeping the schedule
RNG shared, builds \ttk{wandb} on rank $0$ only, and destroys the group in a \ttk{finally} (no barrier
there --- the normal path already barriers, so a mid-step exception cannot deadlock teardown); and the
SLURM launcher gains an \ttk{NRANKS} knob that switches the policy job to
\ttk{torchrun -\/-standalone -\/-nproc\_per\_node=NRANKS} and scales its GPUs/CPUs/memory (with the
reward pool scaled $\sim\!N\times$). Verified: a CPU/\ttk{gloo} gradient-equivalence test
(\ttk{torch.multiprocessing.spawn}, two ranks) asserts the reduced-and-scaled gradient equals the
single-process $\sum\nabla/\sum n$ to \ttk{atol}$=10^{-6}$, covering the uneven $3$-vs-$2$ split and an
all-flat rank; a pure-logic test asserts the shards are a disjoint cover whose union is the serial
list and that \ttk{world\_size}$=1$ reproduces it exactly; and a \ttk{torchrun} smoke test checks the
two-rank process-group all-reduce round-trip. \ttk{ruff} clean. This is a pure wall-clock lever --- it
does not touch reward math or config semantics --- so it composes with all three reward sets above
unchanged, giving an $\approx\!W\times$ faster step on the $8\times$b200 nodes the runs already use.

\paragraph{Remaining (steps 2--4 --- the interface work).}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Step 2 (pure extraction):} carve \ttk{stack.py} (\ttk{RewardStack}: the weighted,
  per-metric-clipped scalar$+$per-token merge) and \ttk{trainers/grpo.py} (\ttk{GRPOConfig}, the
  optimization half of \ttk{GRPOTrainerConfig}) out of \ttk{\_leflur\_grpo\_trainer.py}; move
  \ttk{sft.py} (the CHORD $\phi$-weighted $\mu$-blend) out of \ttk{\_structure\_sft.py}. These are
  still pure moves, but they split a live file, so they carry regression-test risk the Step-1 moves
  did not.
\item \textbf{Step 3 (the only real interface work):} define \ttk{api.py}
  (\ttk{Policy}/\ttk{Trajectory}/\ttk{Reward}/\ttk{RewardPool} protocols), add the
  \ttk{AutoregressivePolicy} log-prob mixin alongside the DFM reference, and refactor
  \ttk{LeFlurGRPOTrainer} to call the protocol via \ttk{adapters/leflur.py} --- regression-tested
  against the live run for byte-identical rewards and \ttk{ratio\_init}$\approx1$.
\item \textbf{Step 4 (packaging):} declare the remaining optional-extras dependency groups
  (\ttk{[ligandmpnn]}, \ttk{[protenix]}, \ttk{[trl]}) so the core installs with torch alone and an
  oracle-backed term raises a clear install error at construction, and write the \ttk{README} with
  the two BYO-model examples above. (\ttk{[tmol]} is already declared, as the ddG reward above
  ships with it.)
\end{itemize}
